\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq (1 - \eta) ^ {k} \frac {4 \left(t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m _ {1} d _ {2} + d _ {3}\right),
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 (t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2})}{\gamma k} + 4 \gamma (d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m _ {1} d _ {2} + d _ {3})
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ , where  $\eta = \min \left\{\gamma \mu /2,\rho_1 / 4,\rho_2 / 4\right\}$ ,  $t^k\stackrel {def}{=}\| \tilde{x}^k -x^*\| ^2 +m_1\gamma^2\sigma_{1,k}^2 +m_2\gamma^2\sigma_{2,k}^2$  and

$$
\begin{array}{l} f _ {1} = \frac {2 4 l \gamma^ {2}}{\delta \rho_ {1} (1 - \eta)} \left(\frac {2 b _ {1}}{\delta} + \widetilde {b} _ {1}\right), \quad f _ {2} = \frac {2 4 l \gamma^ {2}}{\delta \rho_ {2} (1 - \eta)} \left(\frac {2 g}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \left(\frac {2 b _ {1}}{\delta} + \widetilde {b} _ {1}\right) + \frac {2 b _ {2}}{\delta} + \widetilde {b} _ {2}\right), \\ d _ {3} = \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \left(\frac {d _ {2}}{\rho_ {1}} \left(\frac {2 b _ {1}}{\delta} + \widetilde {b} _ {1}\right) + \frac {2 d _ {1}}{\delta} + \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

# h sgd with delayed updates

in this section we consider the sgd with delayed updates (d-sgd) [1, 33, 10, 3, 45]. this method has updates of the form (4)-(5) with

$$
g ^ {k} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k}
$$

$$
v ^ {k} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} v _ {i} ^ {k}, \quad v _ {i} ^ {k} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \gamma g _ {i} ^ {k - \tau}, & \text {i f} t \geq \tau , \\ 0, & \text {i f} t <   \tau \end{array} \right. \tag {70}
$$

$$
e ^ {k} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} e _ {i} ^ {k}, \quad e _ {i} ^ {k + 1} = e _ {i} ^ {k} + \gamma g _ {i} ^ {k} - v _ {i} ^ {k} = \gamma \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} g _ {i} ^ {k + 1 - t}, \tag {71}
$$

where the summation is performed only for non-negative indices. moreover, we assume that  $e_i^0 = 0$  for  $i = 1, \ldots, n$ .

for convenience we also introduce new constant:

$$
\hat {a} = a ^ {\prime} + l \tau . \tag {72}
$$

lemma h.1. let assumptions 3.1 and 3.2 be satisfied, inequalities (15), (16) and (17) hold and

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2 \tau \mu}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {l \tau \left(\hat {a} + \frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} c _ {1}}{\rho_ {1} (1 - \rho_ {1})} + \frac {2 b _ {2} ^ {\prime} c _ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {2})} + \frac {4 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} g c _ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {1}) (1 - \rho_ {2})}\right)}} \right\}, \tag {73}
$$

where  $m_1 = \frac{4b_1'}{3\rho_1}$  and  $m_2 = \frac{4\left(b_2' + \frac{4}{3}g\right)}{3\rho_2}$ . then d-sgd satisfies assumption 3.4, i.e., inequality (19) holds with the following parameters:

$$
f _ {1} = \frac {6 \gamma^ {2} l b _ {1} ^ {\prime} \tau (2 + \rho_ {1})}{\rho_ {1}}, \quad f _ {2} = \frac {6 \gamma^ {2} \tau l (2 + \rho_ {2})}{\rho_ {2}} \left(\frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} g}{1 - \rho_ {1}} + b _ {2} ^ {\prime}\right), \tag {74}
$$

$$
d _ {3} = 3 \gamma \tau l \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} d _ {2}}{\rho_ {1}}\right). \tag {75}
$$

proof. first of all, we derive an upper bound for the second moment of  $e_i^k$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \| e ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \quad \stackrel {(7 1)} {=} \quad \gamma^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} g ^ {k - t} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 6)} {\leq} \quad \gamma^ {2} \tau \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f (x ^ {k - t}) \right\| ^ {2} \right] + \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| g ^ {k - t} - \nabla f (x ^ {k - t}) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 4)} {\leq} \quad \gamma^ {2} \tau \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f (x ^ {k - t}) \right\| ^ {2} \right] + \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| g ^ {k - t} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 5), (1 1)} {\leq} \quad 2 \gamma^ {2} \underbrace {(a ^ {\prime} + l \tau)} _ {\bar {a}} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k - t}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + \gamma^ {2} b _ {1} ^ {\prime} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {1, k - t} ^ {2} \\ + \gamma^ {2} b _ {2} ^ {\prime} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, k - t} ^ {2} + \gamma^ {2} \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime} \tag {76} \\ \end{array}
$$

which implies

$$
\begin{array}{l} {3 l \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \| e ^ {k} \| ^ {2}} {\overset {(7 6)} {\leq}} {6 \gamma^ {2} l \hat {a} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k - t}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right]} \\ + 3 \gamma^ {2} l b _ {1} ^ {\prime} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {1, k - t} ^ {2} \\ + 3 \gamma^ {2} l b _ {2} ^ {\prime} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, k - t} ^ {2} + 3 \gamma^ {2} \tau l d _ {1} ^ {\prime} w _ {k} \tag {77} \\ \end{array}
$$

in the remaining part of the proof we derive upper bounds for four terms in the right-hand side of the previous inequality. first of all, recall that  $w_{k} = (1 - \eta)^{-(k + 1)}$  and  $\eta = \min \left\{\frac{\gamma\mu}{2},\frac{\rho_1}{4},\frac{\rho_2}{4}\right\}$ . it implies that for all  $0\leq i < k$  and  $0\leq t\leq \tau$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} w _ {k} = (1 - \eta) ^ {- (k - t + 1)} (1 - \eta) ^ {- t} \stackrel {(3 2)} {\leq} w _ {k - t} (1 + 2 \eta) ^ {t} \\ \leq w _ {k - t} \left(1 + \gamma \mu\right) ^ {t} \stackrel {(7 3)} {\leq} w _ {k - t} \left(1 + \frac {1}{2 \tau}\right) ^ {t} \leq w _ {k - t} \exp \left(\frac {t}{2 \tau}\right) \leq 2 w _ {k - t}, \tag {78} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
w _ {k} = (1 - \eta) ^ {- (k - j + 1)} (1 - \eta) ^ {- j} \stackrel {(3 2)} {\leq} w _ {k - j} (1 + 2 \eta) ^ {j} \leq w _ {k - j} \left(1 + \frac {\min  \left\{\rho_ {1} , \rho_ {2} \right\}}{2}\right) ^ {j}. \tag {79}
$$

for simplicity, we introduce new notation:  $r_k \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{e}\left[f(x^k) - f(x^*)\right]$ . using this we get

$$
\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} w _ {k} r _ {k - t} \stackrel {(7 8)} {\leq} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} 2 w _ {k - t} r _ {k - t} \leq 2 \tau \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k} \tag {80}
$$

similarly, we estimate the second term in the right-hand side of (79):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {1, k - t} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} 2 w _ {k - t} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {1, k - t} ^ {2} \leq 2 \tau \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(6 4)} {\leq} \quad 2 \tau \sigma_ {1, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k} + \frac {4 c _ {1} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k - l} r _ {l} \\ + \frac {2 g \rho_ {1} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k - l} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, l} ^ {2} + \frac {2 \tau d _ {2}}{\rho} w _ {k}. \tag {81} \\ \end{array}
$$

for the first term in the right-hand side of previous inequality we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} 2 \tau \sigma_ {1, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k} \stackrel {(7 9)} {\leq} 2 \tau \sigma_ {1, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 + \frac {\rho_ {1}}{2}\right) ^ {k + 1} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k} \\ \stackrel {(3 3)} {\leq} \quad 2 \tau \left(1 + \frac {\rho_ {1}}{2}\right) \sigma_ {1, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {1}}{2}\right) ^ {k} \\ \leq \tau \left(2 + \rho_ {1}\right) \sigma_ {1, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {1}}{2}\right) ^ {k} \leq \frac {2 \tau \left(2 + \rho_ {1}\right) \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2}}{\rho_ {1}}. \tag {82} \\ \end{array}
$$

the second term in the right-hand side of (81) can be upper bounded in the following way:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {4 c _ {1} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k - l} r _ {l} \stackrel {(7 9)} {\leq} \frac {4 c _ {1} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {l} r _ {l} \left(1 + \frac {\rho_ {1}}{2}\right) ^ {k - l} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k - l} \\ \stackrel {(3 3)} {\leq} \frac {4 c _ {1} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {l} r _ {l} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {1}}{2}\right) ^ {k - l} \\ \leq \frac {4 c _ {1} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \left(\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k}\right) \left(\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {1}}{2}\right) ^ {k}\right) \\ \leq \frac {8 c _ {1} \tau}{\rho_ {1} (1 - \rho_ {1})} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k}. \tag {83} \\ \end{array}
$$

repeating similar steps we estimate the third term in the right-hand side of (81):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {2 g \rho_ {1} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k - l} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, l} ^ {2} \leq \frac {4 g \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(6 2)} {\leq} \quad \frac {4 g \tau \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {2}) ^ {k} \\ + \frac {8 g c _ {2}}{(1 - \rho_ {1}) (1 - \rho_ {2})} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {2}) ^ {k - l} r _ {l} \\ \stackrel {(7 9)} {\leq} \frac {4 g \tau \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 + \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k + 1} (1 - \rho_ {2}) ^ {k} \\ + \frac {8 g c _ {2} \tau}{(1 - \rho_ {1}) (1 - \rho_ {2})} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 + \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k - l} (1 - \rho_ {2}) ^ {k - l} w _ {l} r _ {l} \\ \stackrel {(3 3)} {\leq} \frac {2 g \tau (2 + \rho_ {2}) \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k} \\ + \frac {8 g c _ {2} \tau}{(1 - \rho_ {1}) (1 - \rho_ {2})} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k - l} w _ {l} r _ {l} \\ \leq \frac {4 g \tau (2 + \rho_ {2}) \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {1})} \\ + \frac {8 g c _ {2} \tau}{(1 - \rho_ {1}) (1 - \rho_ {2})} \left(\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k}\right) \left(\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k}\right) \\ = \frac {4 g \tau (2 + \rho_ {2}) \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {1})} \\ + \frac {1 6 g c _ {2} \tau}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {1}) (1 - \rho_ {2})} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k} \tag {84} \\ \end{array}
$$

combining inequalities (81), (82), (83) and (84) we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {1, k - t} ^ {2} \leq \frac {2 \tau (2 + \rho_ {1}) \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2}}{\rho_ {1}} + \frac {8 \tau}{1 - \rho_ {1}} \left(\frac {c _ {1}}{\rho_ {1}} + \frac {2 g c _ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {2})}\right) \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k} \\ + \frac {4 g \tau (2 + \rho_ {2}) \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {1})} + \frac {2 \tau d _ {2}}{\rho} w _ {k}. \tag {85} \\ \end{array}
$$

next, we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, k - t} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} 2 w _ {k - t} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, k - t} ^ {2} \leq 2 \tau \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(6 2)} {\leq} \quad 2 \tau \sigma_ {2, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {1}) ^ {k} \\ + \frac {4 c _ {2} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {2}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} \left(1 - \rho_ {2}\right) ^ {k - l} r _ {l}. \tag {86} \\ \end{array}
$$

for the first term in the right-hand side of previous inequality we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} 2 \tau \sigma_ {2, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {2}) ^ {k} \stackrel {(7 9)} {\leq} 2 \tau \sigma_ {2, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 + \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k + 1} (1 - \rho_ {2}) ^ {k} \\ \stackrel {(3 3)} {\leq} \quad 2 \tau \left(1 + \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) \sigma_ {2, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k} \\ \leq \tau \left(2 + \rho_ {2}\right) \sigma_ {2, 0} ^ {2} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k} \leq \frac {2 \tau \left(2 + \rho_ {2}\right) \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}{\rho_ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

the second term in the right-hand side of (86) can be upper bounded in the following way:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {4 c _ {2} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {2}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} (1 - \rho_ {2}) ^ {k - l} r _ {l} \stackrel {(7 9)} {\leq} \frac {4 c _ {2} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {2}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {l} r _ {l} \left(1 + \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k - l} (1 - \rho_ {2}) ^ {k - l} \\ \stackrel {(3 3)} {\leq} \frac {4 c _ {2} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {2}} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {l = 0} ^ {k} w _ {l} r _ {l} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k - l} \\ \leq \frac {4 c _ {2} \tau}{1 - \rho_ {2}} \left(\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k}\right) \left(\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(1 - \frac {\rho_ {2}}{2}\right) ^ {k}\right) \\ \leq \frac {8 c _ {2} \tau}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {2})} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k}, \\ \end{array}
$$

hence

$$
\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \sigma_ {2, k - t} ^ {2} \stackrel {(8 6)} {\leq} \frac {2 \tau (2 + \rho_ {2}) \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}{\rho_ {2}} + \frac {8 c _ {2} \tau}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {2})} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k}. \tag {87}
$$

plugging inequalities (80), (85) and (87) in (77) we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} 3 l \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \| e ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq 1 2 \gamma^ {2} l \tau \left(\hat {a} + \frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} c _ {1}}{\rho_ {1} (1 - \rho_ {1})} + \frac {2 b _ {2} ^ {\prime} c _ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {2})} + \frac {4 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} g c _ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {1}) (1 - \rho_ {2})}\right) \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k} \\ + \frac {6 \gamma^ {2} l b _ {1} ^ {\prime} \tau (2 + \rho_ {1})}{\rho_ {1}} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} + \frac {6 \gamma^ {2} \tau l (2 + \rho_ {2})}{\rho_ {2}} \left(\frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} g}{1 - \rho_ {1}} + b _ {2} ^ {\prime}\right) \sigma_ {2, 0} ^ {2} \\ + 3 \gamma^ {2} \tau l \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} d _ {2}}{\rho}\right) w _ {k}. \\ \end{array}
$$

taking into account that  $\gamma \leq \frac{1}{4\sqrt{4l\tau\left(\hat{a} + \frac{2b_1'c_1}{\rho_1(1 - \rho_1)} + \frac{2b_2'c_2}{\rho_2(1 - \rho_2)} + \frac{4b_1'gc_2}{\rho_2(1 - \rho_1)(1 - \rho_2)}\right)}}$ $f_{1} =$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {6 \gamma^ {2} l b _ {1} ^ {\prime} \tau (2 + \rho_ {1})}{\rho_ {1}}, f _ {2} = \frac {6 \gamma^ {2} \tau l}{\rho_ {2}} \left(\frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} g (2 + \rho_ {2})}{1 - \rho_ {1}} + b _ {2} ^ {\prime}\right) \text {a n d} d _ {3} = 3 \gamma \tau l \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} d _ {2}}{\rho}\right) \text {w e g e t a}. \\ 3 l \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} \mathbf {e} \| e ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{4} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} w _ {k} r _ {k} + f _ {1} \sigma_ {1, 0} ^ {2} + f _ {2} \sigma_ {2, 0} ^ {2} + \gamma d _ {3}. \\ \end{array}
$$

□

as a direct application of lemma h.1 and theorem 3.1 we get the following result.

theorem h.1. let assumptions 3.1 and 3.2 be satisfied, inequalities (15), (16) and (17) hold and

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 (a ^ {\prime} + c _ {1} m _ {1} + c _ {2} m _ {2})}, \frac {1}{2 \tau \mu}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {l \tau (\hat {a} + \frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} c _ {1}}{\rho_ {1} (1 - \rho_ {1})} + \frac {2 b _ {2} ^ {\prime} c _ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {2})} + \frac {4 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} g c _ {2}}{\rho_ {2} (1 - \rho_ {1}) (1 - \rho_ {2})})}} \right\},
$$

where  $m_1 = \frac{4b_1'}{3\rho_1}$  and  $m_2 = \frac{4\left(b_2' + \frac{4}{3}g\right)}{3\rho_2}$ . then for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq (1 - \eta) ^ {k} \frac {4 (t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2})}{\gamma} + 4 \gamma (d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m d _ {2} + d _ {3})
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 (t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2})}{\gamma k} + 4 \gamma (d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m d _ {2} + d _ {3})
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ , where  $\eta = \min \left\{\frac{\gamma\mu}{2},\frac{\rho_1}{4},\frac{\rho_2}{4}\right\}$ ,  $t^k \stackrel{def}{=} \| \tilde{x}^k - x^* \|^2 + m_1\gamma^2\sigma_{1,k}^2 + m_2\gamma^2\sigma_{2,k}^2$  and

$$
f _ {1} = \frac {6 \gamma^ {2} l b _ {1} ^ {\prime} \tau (2 + \rho_ {1})}{\rho_ {1}}, f _ {2} = \frac {6 \gamma^ {2} \tau l (2 + \rho_ {2})}{\rho_ {2}} \left(\frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} g}{1 - \rho_ {1}} + b _ {2} ^ {\prime}\right),
$$

$$
d _ {3} = 3 \gamma \tau l \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {2 b _ {1} ^ {\prime} d _ {2}}{\rho_ {1}}\right).
$$

algorithm 1 dianasr with double compression (dianasr-dq)  
input: learning rates  $\gamma >0$ $\alpha \in (0,1]$  , initial vectors  $x^0,h_1^0,\dots ,h_n^0\in \mathbb{r}^d$    
1: set  $h^0 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}h_i^0$    
2: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do   
3: broadcast  $g^{k - 1}$  to all workers  $\triangleright$  if  $k = 0$  , then broadcast  $x^0$    
4: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do   
5:  $x^{k} = x^{k - 1} - \gamma g^{k - 1}$ $\triangleright$  ignore this line if  $k = 0$    
6: sample  $g_{i}^{k,1} = \nabla f_{\xi_{i}^{k}}(x^{k})$  satisfying assumption i.1 independently from other workers   
7:  $\hat{\delta}_i^k = g_i^{k,1} - h_i^k$    
8: sample  $\delta_i^k\sim q_1(\hat{\delta}_i^k)$  independently from other workers   
9:  $g_{i}^{k,2} = h_{i}^{k} + \delta_{i}^{k}$    
10:  $h_i^{k + 1} = h_i^k +\alpha \delta_i^k$    
11: end for   
12:  $g^{k,2} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k,2} = h^{k} + \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\delta_{i}^{k}$    
13:  $h^{k + 1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}h_{i}^{k + 1} = h^{k} + \alpha \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\delta_{i}^{k}$    
14: sample  $g^{k}\sim q_{2}(g^{k,2})$    
15:  $x^{k + 1} = x^{k} - \gamma g^{k - 1}$    
16: end for

# i special cases: sgd

to illustrate the generality of our approach, we develop and analyse a new special case of sgd without error-feedback and show that in some cases, our framework recovers tighter rates than the framework from [11].

# i.1 diana with arbitrary sampling and double quantization

in this section we consider problem (1) with  $f(x)$  being  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex and  $f_{i}(x)$  satisfying (3) where functions  $f_{ij}(x)$  are differentiable, but not necessary convex. following [13] we construct a stochastic reformulation of this problem:

$$
f (x) = \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} [ f _ {\xi} (x) ], \quad f _ {\xi} (x) = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x), \quad f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) = \frac {1}{m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \xi_ {i j} f _ {i j} (x), \qquad (8 8)
$$

where  $\xi = (\xi_1^\top, \ldots, \xi_n^\top), \xi_i = (\xi_{i1}, \ldots, \xi_{im})^\top$  is a random vector with distribution  $\mathcal{d}_i$  such that  $\mathbf{e}_{\mathcal{d}_i}[\xi_{ij}] = 1$  for all  $i \in [n], j \in [m]$  and the following assumption holds.

assumption i.1 (expected smoothness). we assume that functions  $f_{1},\ldots ,f_{n}$  are  $\mathcal{l}$ -smooth in expectation w.r.t. distributions  $\mathcal{d}_1,\dots ,\mathcal{d}_n$ , i.e., there exists constant  $\mathcal{l} = \mathcal{l}(f,\mathcal{d}_1,\dots ,\mathcal{d}_n)$  such that

$$
\mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 2 \mathcal {l} d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x, x ^ {*}\right) \tag {89}
$$

for all  $i\in [n]$  and  $x\in \mathbb{r}^d$

to solve this problem, we consider diana [37, 19] — a distributed stochastic method using unbiased compressions or quantizations for communication between workers and master. we start with the formal definition of quantization. in [37, 19] diana was analyzed under the assumption that stochastic gradients  $g_{i}^{k}$  have uniformly bounded variances which is not very practical.

therefore, we consider a slightly different method called dianasr-dq which works with the stochastic reformulation (88) of problem  $(1)+(3)$ , see algorithm 1. moreover, to illustrate the flexibility of our approach, we consider compression not only on the workers' side but also on the master side. to perform an update of dianasr-dq master needs to gather quantized gradient differences  $\delta_{i}^{k}$  and the to broadcast quantized stochastic gradient  $g^{k}$  to all workers. clearly, in this case, only compressed vectors participate in communication.

in the concurrent work [40] the same method was independently proposed under the name of artemis. however, our analysis is slightly more general: it is based on assumption i.1 while in [40] authors assume  $l$ -cocoercivity of stochastic gradients almost surely. next, a very similar approach was considered in [47], where authors present a method with error compensation on master and worker sides. moreover, recently another method called dore was developed in [35], which uses diana-trick on the worker side and error compensation on the master side. however, in these methods, compression operators are the same on both sides, despite the fact that gathering the information often costs much more than broadcasting. therefore, the natural idea is in using different quantization for gathering and broadcasting, and it is what dianasr-dq does. moreover, we do not assume uniform boundedness of the second moment of the stochastic gradient like in [47], and we also do not assume uniform boundedness of the variance of the stochastic gradient like in [35]. assumption i.1 is more natural and always holds for the problems (1)+(3) when  $f_{ij}$  are convex and  $l$ -smooth for each  $i \in [n]$ ,  $j \in [m]$ . in contrast, in the same setup, there exist such problems that the variance of the stochastic gradients is not uniformly upper bounded by any finite constant.

we assume that  $q_{1}$  and  $q_{2}$  satisfy (26) with parameters  $\omega_{1}$  and  $\omega_{2}$  respectively.

lemma i.1. let assumption i.1 be satisfied. then, for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \nabla f (x ^ {k}), \tag {90}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 2 \mathcal {l} \left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right) \left(2 + \frac {3 \omega_ {1}}{n}\right) \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {3 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + d _ {1,} ^ {\prime} (9 1)
$$

$$
w h e r e \quad \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \quad = \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} \quad - \quad \nabla f (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \quad a n d \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} \quad =
$$

$$
\frac {(2 + 3 \omega_ {1}) (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right].
$$

proof. first of all, we show inbiasedness of  $g^k$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \stackrel {(3 5), (2 6)} {=} \mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k, 2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = h ^ {k} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \delta_ {i} ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 5), (2 6)} {=} h ^ {k} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \hat {\delta} _ {i} ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ = \quad h ^ {k} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k}\right) = \nabla f \left(x ^ {k}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

next, to denote mathematical expectation w.r.t. the randomness coming from quantizations  $q_{1}$  and  $q_{2}$  at iteration  $k$  we use  $\mathbf{e}_{q_1^k}[\cdot ]$  and  $\mathbf{e}_{q_2^k}[\cdot ]$  respectively. using these notations and the definition of quantization we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} _ {q _ {2} ^ {k}} [ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} ] \stackrel {(3 4), (2 6)} {=} \| g ^ {k, 1} \| ^ {2} + \mathbf {e} _ {q _ {2} ^ {k}} \left[ \| g ^ {k, 2} - g ^ {k, 1} \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(2 6)} {\leq} \quad (1 + \omega_ {2}) \| g ^ {k, 1} \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

taking the conditopnal mathematical expectation  $\mathbf{e}_{q_1^k}[\cdot ]$  from the both sides of previous inequality and using the independence of  $\delta_i^1,\ldots ,\delta_i^n$  we get

$$
\mathbf {e} _ {q _ {1} ^ {k}, q _ {2} ^ {k}} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {{(3 5)}} {{=}} (1 + \omega_ {2}) \mathbf {e} _ {q _ {1} ^ {k}} \left[ \| g ^ {k, 1} \| ^ {2} \right] = (1 + \omega_ {2}) \mathbf {e} _ {q _ {1} ^ {k}} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (h _ {i} ^ {k} + \delta_ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\stackrel {(3 4)} {=} \qquad (1 + \omega_ {2}) \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(h _ {i} ^ {k} + \hat {\delta} _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} + (1 + \omega_ {2}) \mathbf {e} _ {q _ {1} ^ {k}} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (\delta_ {i} ^ {k} - \hat {\delta} _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \left. \left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right) \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) + \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right. \\ + \frac {\left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right)}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {q _ {1} ^ {k}} \left[ \| \delta_ {i} ^ {k} - \hat {\delta} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(3 1), (2 6)} {\leq} \frac {2 (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + 2 \left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right) \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {\omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2 (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + 2 \left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right) \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {3 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {3 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {3 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, we take conditional mathematical expectation  $\mathbf{e}[\cdot \mid x^k ]$  from the both sides of the inequality above and use the independence of  $\xi_1^k,\ldots ,\xi_n^k$  ..

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \stackrel {(8 9)} {\leq} 2 \mathcal {l} (1 + \omega_ {2}) \left(2 + \frac {3 \omega_ {1}}{n}\right) (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})) + \frac {3 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \\ + 2 (1 + \omega_ {2}) \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} | x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {3 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ = 2 \mathcal {l} \left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right) \left(2 + \frac {3 \omega_ {1}}{n}\right) \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {3 \omega_ {1} \left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right)}{n} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \\ + \frac {(1 + \omega_ {2}) (2 + 3 \omega_ {1})}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

□

lemma i.2. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth, assumption i.1 holds and  $\alpha \leq 1 / (\omega_1 + 1)$ . then, for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - \alpha) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 \alpha \left(3 \mathcal {l} + 4 l\right) \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + d _ {2}, \tag {92}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|h_i^k - \nabla f_i(x^*)\|^2$  and  $d_2 = \frac{3\alpha}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{e}_{\mathcal{d}_i}\left[\|\nabla f_{\xi_i}(x^*) - \nabla f_i(x^*)\|^2\right]$ .

proof. for simplicity, we introduce new notation:  $h_i^* \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \nabla f_i(x^*)$ . using this we derive an upper bound for the second moment of  $h_i^{k+1} - h_i^*$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| h _ {i} ^ {k + 1} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} + \alpha \delta_ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(2 6)} {=} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} + 2 \alpha \langle h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*}, \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \rangle + \alpha^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \delta_ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(2 6), (3 5)} {\leq} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} + 2 \alpha \langle h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*}, \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \rangle \\ + \alpha^ {2} \left(\omega_ {1} + 1\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

using variance decomposition (34) and  $\alpha \leq 1 / (\omega_1 + 1)$  we get

$$
\alpha^ {2} \left(\omega_ {1} + 1\right) \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \quad \stackrel {(3 4)} {=} \quad \alpha^ {2} \left(\omega_ {1} + 1\right) \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) \| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} + \alpha^ {2} \left(\omega_ {1} + 1\right) \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad 3 \alpha \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + 3 \alpha \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + 3 \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \\ + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 1), (8 9)} {\leq} \quad 6 \alpha (\mathcal {l} + l) d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ + 3 \alpha \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

putting all together we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| h _ {i} ^ {k + 1} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \alpha \left\langle \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k}, f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) + h _ {i} ^ {k} - 2 h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\rangle \\ + 6 \alpha (\mathcal {l} + l) d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) + 3 \alpha \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(2 8)} {=} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} - \alpha \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \\ + 6 \alpha (\mathcal {l} + l) d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) + 3 \alpha \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} \quad (1 - \alpha) \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \alpha (6 \mathcal {l} + 8 l) d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \\ + 3 \alpha \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

summing up the above inequality for  $i = 1, \ldots, n$  we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| h _ {i} ^ {k + 1} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq \frac {1 - \alpha}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \alpha (6 \mathcal {l} + 8 l) (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})) \\ + \frac {3 \alpha}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

□

theorem i.1. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$ ,  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex and assumption i.1 holds. then dianasr-dq satisfies assumption 3.4 with

$$
a ^ {\prime} = \mathcal {l} (1 + \omega_ {2}) \left(2 + \frac {3 \omega_ {1}}{n}\right), \quad b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {3 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n},
$$

$$
d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {(2 + 3 \omega_ {1}) (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right],
$$

$$
\sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} = \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 0, \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0, \rho_ {1} = \alpha , \rho_ {2} = 1,
$$

$$
c _ {1} = \alpha (3 \mathcal {l} + 4 l), \quad c _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {2} = \frac {3 \alpha}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right],
$$

$$
g = 0, \quad f _ {1} = f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = 0,
$$

with  $\gamma$  and  $\alpha$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \frac {1}{4 (1 + \omega_ {2}) \left(\mathcal {l} \left(2 + \frac {1 5 \omega_ {1}}{n}\right) + \frac {1 6 l \omega_ {1}}{n}\right)}, \quad \alpha \leq \frac {1}{\omega + 1}, \quad m _ {1} = \frac {4 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n \alpha}, \quad m _ {2} = 0
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2}, \frac {\alpha}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 t ^ {0}}{\gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m _ {1} d _ {2}\right),
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 t ^ {0}}{\gamma k} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m _ {1} d _ {2}\right)
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ , where  $t^k \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \|x^k - x^*\|^2 + m_1 \gamma^2 \sigma_{1,k}^2$ .

in other words, if

$$
\gamma = \frac {1}{4 \left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right) \left(\mathcal {l} \left(2 + \frac {1 5 \omega_ {1}}{n}\right) + \frac {1 6 l \omega_ {1}}{n}\right)}, \quad \alpha = \frac {1}{\omega + 1}
$$

and  $d_{1} = 0$ , i.e.,  $\nabla f_{\xi_i^k}(x^k) = \nabla f_i(x^k)$  almost surely, dianasr-dq converges with the linear rate

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\left(\omega_ {1} + \frac {\mathcal {l}}{\mu} (1 + \omega_ {2}) \left(1 + \frac {\omega_ {1}}{n}\right)\right) \ln \frac {1}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

to the exact solution. applying lemma d.2 we establish the rate of convergence to  $\varepsilon$ -solution. corollary i.1. let the assumptions of theorem i.1 hold and  $\mu > 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of dianasq-dq with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \frac {1}{4 (1 + \omega_ {2}) \left(\mathcal {l} \left(2 + \frac {1 5 \omega_ {1}}{n}\right) + \frac {1 6 l \omega_ {1}}{n}\right)}
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \frac {\mu^ {2} k ^ {2} (\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + m _ {1} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2})}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m _ {1} d _ {2}} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}, m _ {1} = \frac {4 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n \alpha}
$$

and  $\alpha = \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(a ^ {\prime} \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {\mu}{a ^ {\prime}}, \frac {1}{\omega_ {1}} \right\} k\right) + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m _ {1} d _ {2}}{\mu k}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  dianasq-dq requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\omega_ {1} + \frac {\mathcal {l} \left(1 + \frac {\omega_ {1}}{n}\right) (1 + \omega_ {2})}{\mu} + \frac {(1 + \omega_ {1}) (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n ^ {2} \mu \varepsilon} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary i.2. let the assumptions of theorem i.1 hold and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of dianasq-dq with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \frac {1}{4 \left(1 + \omega_ {2}\right) \left(\mathcal {l} \left(2 + \frac {1 5 \omega_ {1}}{n}\right) + \frac {1 6 l \omega_ {1}}{n}\right)}
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{m _ {1} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt {\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + m _ {1} d _ {2}) k}} \right\}, m _ {1} = \frac {4 \omega_ {1} (1 + \omega_ {2})}{n \alpha}
$$

and  $\alpha = \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\mathcal {l} r _ {0} ^ {2} (1 + \omega_ {2}) \left(1 + \frac {\omega_ {1}}{n}\right)}{k} + \frac {r _ {0} \sigma_ {1 , 0} (1 + \omega_ {1}) \sqrt {1 + \omega_ {2}}}{\sqrt {n} k} + \frac {r _ {0} \sqrt {(1 + \omega_ {1}) (1 + \omega_ {2}) d _ {o p t}}}{\sqrt {n k}}\right)
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\| ^2,d_{opt} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\mathbf{e}_{\mathcal{d}_i}\| \nabla f_{\xi_i}(x^*) - \nabla f_i(x^*)\| ^2$  . that is, to achieve

$\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  dianasq-dq requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\mathcal {l} r _ {0} ^ {2} (1 + \omega_ {2}) \left(1 + \frac {\omega_ {1}}{n}\right)}{\varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} \sigma_ {1 , 0} (1 + \omega_ {1}) \sqrt {1 + \omega_ {2}}}{\sqrt {n} \varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} (1 + \omega_ {1}) (1 + \omega_ {2}) d _ {o p t}}{n \varepsilon^ {2}}\right)
$$

iterations.

# i.2 recovering tight complexity bounds for vr-diana

in this section we consider the same problem (1)+(3) and variance reduced version of diana called vr-diana [19], see algorithm 2. for simplicity we assume that each  $f_{ij}$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth and  $f_{i}$  is additionally  $\mu$ -strongly convex.

lemma 1.3 (lemmas 3, 5, 6 and 7 from [19]). let  $\alpha \leq \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$ . then for all iterates  $k \geq 0$  of algorithm 2 the following inequalities hold:

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \nabla f \left(x ^ {k}\right), \tag {93}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ h ^ {k + 1} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - \alpha) h ^ {k} + \frac {2 \alpha}{m} d ^ {k} + 8 \alpha l n \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right), \tag {94}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ d ^ {k + 1} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq \left(1 - \frac {1}{m}\right) d ^ {k} + 2 l n \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right), \tag {95}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 2 l \left(1 + \frac {4 \omega + 2}{n}\right) \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2 \omega}{n ^ {2}} \frac {d ^ {k}}{m} + \frac {2 (\omega + 1)}{n ^ {2}} h ^ {k}, \tag {96}
$$

where  $h^{k} = \sum_{i = 1}^{n}\| h_{i}^{k} - \nabla f_{i}(x^{*})\|^{2}$  and  $d^{k} = \sum_{i = 1}^{n}\sum_{j = 1}^{m}\| \nabla f_{ij}(w_{ij}^{k}) - \nabla f_{ij}(x^{*})\|^{2}$ .

this lemma shows that vr-diana satisfies (15), (16) and (17). applying theorem f.1 we get the following result.

theorem i.2. assume that  $f_{ij}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  and  $f_i(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$ . then vr-diana satisfies assumption 3.4 with

$$
a ^ {\prime} = l \left(1 + \frac {4 \omega + 2}{n}\right), \quad b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2 (\omega + 1)}{n}, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = 0,
$$

$$
\sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} = h ^ {k} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2 \omega}{n},
$$

$$
\sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} = d ^ {k} = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i j} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \rho_ {1} = \alpha , \quad \rho_ {2} = \frac {1}{m},
$$

$$
c _ {1} = 4 \alpha l, \quad c _ {2} = \frac {l}{m}, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \quad g = 2, \quad f _ {1} = f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = 0,
$$

algorithm 2 vr-diana based on lsvrg (variant 1), saga (variant 2), [19]

input: learning rates  $\alpha > 0$  and  $\gamma > 0$ , initial vectors  $x^0, h_1^0, \ldots, h_n^0$ ,  $h^0 = \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} h_i^0$

1: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do

2: sample random  $u^{k} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} 1, & \text{with probability } \frac{1}{m} \\ 0, & \text{with probability } 1 - \frac{1}{m} \end{array} \right.$  ▷ only for variant 1

3: broadcast  $x^{k}$ ,  $u^{k}$  to all workers

4: for  $i = 1,\ldots ,n$  in parallel do  $\triangleright$  worker side

5: pick  $j_i^k$  uniformly at random from  $[m]$

6:  $\mu_{i}^{k} = \frac{1}{m}\sum_{j = 1}^{m}\nabla f_{ij}(w_{ij}^{k})$

7:  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{ij_{i}^{k}}(x^{k}) - \nabla f_{ij_{i}^{k}}(w_{ij_{i}^{k}}^{k}) + \mu_{i}^{k}$

8:  $\hat{\delta}_i^k = q(g_i^k - h_i^k)$

9:  $h_{j}^{k + 1} = h_{j}^{k} + \alpha \hat{\delta}_{j}^{k}$

10: for  $j = 1,\dots ,m$  do

11:  $w_{ij}^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^k, & \mathrm{if} u^k = 1\\ w_{ij}^k, & \mathrm{if} u^k = 0 \end{array} \right.$  ▷ variant 1 (l-svrg): update epoch gradient if

$$
u ^ {k} = 1
$$

12:  $w_{ij}^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^k, & j = j_i^k\\ w_{ij}^k, & j\neq j_i^k \end{array} \right.$  ▷ variant 2 (saga): update gradient table

13: end for  
14: end for  
15:  $h^{k + 1} = h^k +\frac{\alpha}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^n\hat{\delta}_i^k$ $\triangleright$  gather quantized updates  
16:  $g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}(\hat{\delta}_{i}^{k} + h_{i}^{k})$  
17:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -\gamma g^k$  
18: end for

with  $\gamma$  and  $\alpha$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \frac {3}{l \left(\frac {4 1}{3} + \frac {5 2 \omega + 3 5}{n}\right)}, \quad \alpha \leq \frac {1}{\omega + 1}, \quad m _ {1} = \frac {8 (\omega + 1)}{3 n \alpha}, \quad m _ {2} = \frac {8 \omega m}{3 n} + \frac {3 2 m}{9}
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2}, \frac {\alpha}{4}, \frac {1}{4 m} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 t ^ {0}}{\gamma},
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 t ^ {0}}{\gamma k}
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ , where  $t^k \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \|x^k - x^*\|^2 + m_1 \gamma^2 \sigma_{1,k}^2 + m_2 \gamma^2 \sigma_{2,k}^2$ .

in other words, if  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\gamma = \frac {3}{l \left(\frac {4 1}{3} + \frac {5 2 \omega + 3 5}{n}\right)}, \quad \alpha = \frac {1}{\omega + 1},
$$

then vr-diana converges with the linear rate

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\left(\omega + m + \kappa \left(1 + \frac {\omega}{n}\right)\right) \ln \frac {1}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

to the exact solution which coincides with the rate obtained in [19]. we notice that the framework from [11] establishes slightly worse guarantee:

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\left(\omega + m + \kappa \left(1 + \frac {\omega}{n}\right) \frac {\operatorname* {m a x} \{m , \omega + 1 \}}{m}\right) \ln \frac {1}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

this guarantee is strictly worse than our bound when  $m \leq 1 + \omega$ . the key tool that helps us to improve the rate is two sequences of  $\{\sigma_{1,k}^2\}_{k \geq 0}$ ,  $\{\sigma_{2,k}^2\}_{k \geq 0}$  instead of one sequence  $\{\sigma_k^2\}_{k \geq 0}$  as in [11].

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary i.3. let the assumptions of theorem i.2 hold and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of vr-diana with the stepsize

$$
\begin{array}{l} \gamma_ {0} = \frac {3}{l \left(\frac {4 1}{3} + \frac {5 2 \omega + 3 5}{n}\right)} \\ \gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{m _ {1} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + m _ {2} \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}} \right\}, \quad m _ {1} = \frac {8 (\omega + 1)}{3 n \alpha}, \quad m _ {2} = \frac {8 \omega m}{3 n} + \frac {3 2 m}{9} \\ \end{array}
$$

and  $\alpha = \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2} \left(1 + \frac {\omega}{n}\right)}{k} + \frac {r _ {0} \sqrt {\frac {(1 + \omega) ^ {2}}{n} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \left(1 + \frac {\omega}{n}\right) m \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}}{k}\right)
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|^2$ . that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^{k}) - f(x^{*})\right]\leq \varepsilon$  vr-diana requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2} \left(1 + \frac {\omega}{n}\right)}{\varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} \sqrt {\frac {(1 + \omega) ^ {2}}{n} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \left(1 + \frac {\omega}{n}\right) m \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

iterations.

algorithm 3 ec-sgdsr  
input: learning rate  $\gamma >0$  , initial vector  $x^0\in \mathbb{r}^d$    
1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1,\dots ,n$    
2: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do   
3: broadcast  $x^{k}$  to all workers   
4: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do   
5: sample  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{\xi_{i}}(x^{k})$    
6:  $v_{i}^{k} = c(e_{i}^{k} + \gamma g_{i}^{k})$    
7:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$    
8: end for   
9:  $e^k = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^n e_i^k$ $g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k}$ $v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k}$    
10:  $x^{k + 1} = x^{k} - v^{k}$    
11: end for

# j special cases: error compensated methods

# j.1 ec-sgdsr

in this section we consider the same setup as in section i.1 and assume additionally that  $f_{1},\ldots ,f_{n}$  are  $l$ -smooth.

lemma j.1. for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2},
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 6 (\mathcal {l} + l) \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right],
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 \mathcal {l} \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right].
$$

proof. applying straightforward inequality  $\| a + b\|^2 \leq 2\| a\|^2 + 2\| b\|^2$  for  $a, b \in \mathbb{r}^d$  we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) + \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2}. \tag {97} \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \quad = \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1), (8 9)} {\leq} \quad 6 (\mathcal {l} + l) \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

next, using the independence of  $\xi_1^k,\ldots ,\xi_n^k$  we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| g ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) + \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + 2 \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(8 9)} {\leq} \quad 4 \mathcal {l} \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/fc9c2806f8294b5820a7fc3baac37c675b57538c70ae4c9f79cee80eaa7c9f3d.jpg)

applying theorem g.1 we get the following result.

theorem j.1. assume that  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex,  $f_{1},\ldots ,f_{n}$  are  $l$ -smooth and assumption i.1 holds. then ec-sgdsr satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
a = 2 l, \quad \widetilde {a} = 3 (\mathcal {l} + l), \quad a ^ {\prime} = 2 \mathcal {l}, \quad b _ {1} = \widetilde {b} _ {1} = b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} = \widetilde {b} _ {2} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 0,
$$

$$
d _ {1} = \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right], \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \equiv \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0,
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right], \quad \rho_ {1} = \rho_ {2} = 1, \quad c _ {1} = c _ {2} = 0, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \\ f _ {1} = f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \left(\frac {2 d _ {1}}{\delta} + \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{8 \mathcal {l}}, \frac {\delta}{4 \sqrt {6 l (4 l + 3 \delta (\mathcal {l} + l))}} \right\}
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \frac {\gamma \mu}{2}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {1 2 l \gamma}{\delta^ {2}} d _ {1} + \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right)
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{k \gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {1 2 l \gamma}{\delta^ {2}} d _ {1} + \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right)
$$

when  $\mu = 0$

in other words, ec-sgdsr converges with linear rate  $\mathcal{o}\left(\left(\frac{\mathcal{l}}{\mu} +\frac{l + \sqrt{\delta l\mathcal{l}}}{\mu\delta}\right)\ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$  to the neighbourhood of the solution when  $\mu >0$ . applying lemma d.2 we establish the rate of convergence to  $\varepsilon$ -solution.

corollary j.1. let the assumptions of theorem j.1 hold and  $\mu > 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-sgdsr with the stepsize

$$
\gamma = \operatorname * {m i n} \left\{\frac {1}{8 \mathcal {l}}, \frac {\delta}{4 \sqrt {6 l \left(4 l + 3 \delta (\mathcal {l} + l)\right)}}, \frac {\ln \left(\operatorname * {m a x} \left\{2 , \operatorname * {m i n} \left\{\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime}} , \frac {\delta \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{6 l (^ {2 d _ {1}} / \delta + \widetilde {d} _ {1})} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}
$$

we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\left(\mathcal {l} + \frac {l + \sqrt {\delta l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta}\right) \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \exp \left(- \frac {\mu}{\mathcal {l} + \frac {l + \sqrt {\delta l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta}} k\right) + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu k} + \frac {l (\widetilde {d} _ {1} + d _ {1} / \delta)}{\delta \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgdsr requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\mathcal {l}}{\mu} + \frac {l + \sqrt {\delta l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta \mu} + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l (\widetilde {d} _ {1} + ^ {d _ {1}} / \delta)}}{\mu \sqrt {\delta \varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary j.2. let the assumptions of theorem j.1 hold and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-sgdsr with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{8 \mathcal {l}}, \frac {\delta}{4 \sqrt {6 l (4 l + 3 \delta (\mathcal {l} + l))}} \right\}
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime} k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \delta}{6 l (^ {2 d _ {1}} / \delta + \widetilde {d} _ {1}) k}} \right\}
$$

we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \left(\mathcal {l} + \frac {l + \sqrt {\delta l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta}\right)}{k} + \sqrt {\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} \left(^ {2 d _ {1} / \delta} + \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right)}}{\left(\delta k ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 3}}\right)
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|^2$ . that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]\leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgdsr requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \left(\mathcal {l} + \frac {l + \sqrt {\delta l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta}\right)}{\varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l (^ {2 d _ {1} / \delta} + \widetilde {d} _ {1})}}{\sqrt {\delta \varepsilon^ {3}}}\right)
$$

iterations.

# j.2 ec-sgd

in this section we consider problem (1) with  $f_{i}(x)$  satisfying (2) where functions  $f_{\xi_i}(x)$  are differentiable and  $l$ -smooth almost surely in  $\xi_{i}$ ,  $i = 1,\ldots ,n$ .

# algorithm 4 ec-sgd

input: learning rate  $\gamma > 0$ , initial vector  $x^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$

1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  
2: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do  
3: broadcast  $x^k$  to all workers  
4: for  $i = 1,\ldots ,n$  in parallel do  
5: sample  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{\xi_{i}}(x^{k})$  independently from other workers  
6:  $v_{i}^{k} = c(e_{i}^{k} + \gamma g_{i}^{k})$  
7:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$  
8: end for  
9:  $e^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}e_{i}^{k},g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k},v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k}$  
0:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -v^k$

11: end for

lemma j.2 (see also lemmas 1,2 from [39]). assume that  $f_{\xi_i}(x)$  are convex in  $x$  for every  $\xi_i$ ,  $i = 1, \ldots, n$ . then for every  $x \in \mathbb{r}^d$  and  $i = 1, \ldots, n$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x) \| ^ {2} \leq 4 l \left(f (x) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {i} (x) \| ^ {2} \leq 1 2 l \left(f (x) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right], \\ \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {1}, \dots , \xi_ {n}} \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) \right\| ^ {2} \leq 4 l \left(f (x) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

if further  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex with  $\mu > 0$  and possibly non-convex  $f_{i}, f_{\xi_{i}}$ , then for every  $x \in \mathbb{r}^{d}$  and  $i = 1, \ldots, n$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x) \| ^ {2} \leq 4 l \kappa \left(f (x) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {i} (x) \| ^ {2} \leq 1 2 l \kappa \left(f (x) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right], \\ \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {1}, \dots , \xi_ {n}} \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) \right\| ^ {2} \leq 4 l \kappa \left(f (x) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\kappa = \frac{l}{\mu}$ .

proof. we start with the case when functions  $f_{\xi_i}(x)$  are convex in  $x$  for every  $\xi_i$ . the first inequality follows from (97). next, we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {i} (x) \| ^ {2} \quad \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i}} \sim_ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} \quad 1 2 l \left(f (x) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

due to independence of  $\xi_1^k,\ldots ,\xi_n^k$  we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {1}, \dots , \xi_ {n}} \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) \right\| ^ {2} = \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {1}, \dots , \xi_ {n}} \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) + \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + 2 \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {1}, \dots , \xi_ {n}} \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} 4 l \left(f (x) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

next, we consider the second case:  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex with possibly non-convex  $f_{i}$ ,  $f_{\xi_i}$ . in this case

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x) \| ^ {2} \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 0)} {\leq} \quad 2 l ^ {2} \| x - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {4 l ^ {2}}{\mu} (f (x) - f (x ^ {*})) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality follows from  $\mu$ -strong convexity of  $f$ . similarly, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {i} (x) \| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} (x) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 0)} {\leq} \quad 6 l ^ {2} \| x - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \frac {1 2 l ^ {2}}{\mu} (f (x) - f (x ^ {*})) \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, using independence of  $\xi_1^k,\ldots ,\xi_n^k$  we derive

$$
\mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {1}, \ldots , \xi_ {n}} \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) \right\| ^ {2} = \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {1}, \ldots , \xi_ {n}} \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) + \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})) \right\| ^ {2}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} \sim \mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + 2 \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {1}, \dots , \xi_ {n}} \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 0)} {\leq} 2 l ^ {2} \| x - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \frac {4 l ^ {2}}{\mu} (f (x) - f (x ^ {*})) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/312db587aa54fc5e3bb14e3f882c0b141d847a035bb342b3c67f7a7e9e2fe8af.jpg)

applying theorem g.1 we get the following result.

theorem j.2. assume that  $f_{\xi}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth in  $x$  for every  $\xi$  and  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex. then ec-sgd satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
\begin{array}{l} a = a ^ {\prime} = 2 l, \quad \widetilde {a} = 6 l, \quad b _ {1} = \widetilde {b} _ {1} = b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} = \widetilde {b} _ {2} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 0, \\ d _ {1} = \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right], \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \equiv \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0, \\ d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right], \quad \rho_ {1} = \rho_ {2} = 1, \quad c _ {1} = c _ {2} = 0, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \\ f _ {1} = f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \left(\frac {2 d _ {1}}{\delta} + \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {6 + 9 \delta}}
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \frac {\gamma \mu}{2}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {1 2 l \gamma}{\delta^ {2}} d _ {1} + \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right)
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{k \gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {1 2 l \gamma}{\delta^ {2}} d _ {1} + \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right)
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ . if further  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex with  $\mu > 0$  and possibly non-convex  $f_{i}, f_{\xi_{i}}$ , then ec-sgd satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
a = a ^ {\prime} = 2 l \kappa , \quad \widetilde {a} = 6 l \kappa , \quad b _ {1} = \widetilde {b} _ {1} = b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} = \widetilde {b} _ {2} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 0,
$$

$$
d _ {1} = \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right], \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \equiv \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0,
$$

$$
d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right], \quad \rho_ {1} = \rho_ {2} = 1, \quad c _ {1} = c _ {2} = 0, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0,
$$

$$
f _ {1} = f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \left(\frac {2 d _ {1}}{\delta} + \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right),
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{8 \kappa l}, \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {3 \kappa (2 + 3 \delta)}} \right\}
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \frac {\gamma \mu}{2}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \frac {1 2 l \gamma}{\delta^ {2}} d _ {1} + \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right).
$$

in other words, ec-sgd converges with linear rate  $\mathcal{o}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\delta}\ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$  to the neighbourhood of the solution when  $f_{\xi}(x)$  are convex for each  $\xi$  and  $\mu >0$ . applying lemma d.2 we establish the rate of convergence to  $\varepsilon$ -solution.

corollary j.3. let the assumptions of theorem j.2 hold,  $f_{\xi}(x)$  are convex for each  $\xi$  and  $\mu > 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-sgd with the stepsize

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {6 + 9 \delta}}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \min \left\{\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime}} , \frac {\delta \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{6 l (^ {2 d _ {1}} / \delta + \widetilde {d} _ {1})} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}
$$

we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {l}{\delta} \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \exp \left(- \frac {\delta \mu}{l} k\right) + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu k} + \frac {l \left(\widetilde {d} _ {1} + d _ {1} / \delta\right)}{\delta \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd requires

$$
\tilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {l}{\delta \mu} + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \left(\tilde {d} _ {1} + d _ {1} / \delta\right)}}{\mu \sqrt {\delta \varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

corollary j.4. let the assumptions of theorem j.2 hold and  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex with  $\mu > 0$  and possibly non-convex  $f_i, f_{\xi_i}$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-sgd with the stepsize

$$
\gamma = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{8 \kappa l}, \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {3 \kappa (2 + 3 \delta)}}, \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}, \frac {\delta \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{6 l \left(2 d _ {1} / \delta + \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right)} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}
$$

we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\left(l \kappa + \frac {l \sqrt {\kappa}}{\delta}\right) \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {\delta \mu}{l \sqrt {\kappa}}, \frac {1}{\kappa^ {2}} \right\} k\right) + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu k} + \frac {l (\widetilde {d} _ {1} + d _ {1} / \delta)}{\delta \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\kappa^ {2} + \frac {\kappa^ {3 / 2}}{\delta} + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l (\widetilde {d} _ {1} + d _ {1} / \delta)}}{\mu \sqrt {\delta} \varepsilon}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary j.5. let the assumptions of theorem j.2 hold,  $f_{\xi}(x)$  are convex for each  $\xi$  and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-sgd with the stepsize

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {6 + 9 \delta}}, \sqrt {\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime} k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \delta}{6 l (^ {2 d _ {1}} / \delta + \widetilde {d} _ {1}) k}} \right\}
$$

we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta k} + \sqrt {\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} \left(2 d _ {1} / \delta + \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right)}}{\left(\delta k ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 3}}\right)
$$

algorithm 5 ec-gdstar (see also [11])  
input: learning rate  $\gamma >0$  , initial vector  $x^0\in \mathbb{r}^d$    
1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1,\dots ,n$    
2: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do   
3: broadcast  $x^{k}$  to all workers   
4: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do   
5:  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{i}(x^{k}) - \nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$    
6:  $v_{i}^{k} = c(e_{i}^{k} + \gamma g_{i}^{k})$    
7:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$    
8: end for   
9:  $e^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}e_{i}^{k},g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k},v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k}$    
10:  $x^{k + 1} = x^{k} - v^{k}$    
11: end for

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|^2$ . that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^{k}) - f(x^{*})\right]\leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta \varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l \left(^ {2 d _ {1}} / \delta + \widetilde {d} _ {1}\right)}}{\sqrt {\delta \varepsilon^ {3}}}\right)
$$

iterations.

# j.3 ec-gdstar

we assume that  $i$ -th node has access to the gradient of  $f_{i}$  at the optimality, i.e., to the  $\nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$ . it is unrealistic scenario but it gives some insights that we will use next in order to design the method that converges asymptotically to the exact solution.

assume that  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex and each  $f_{i}$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth. by definition of  $g_{i}^{k}$  it trivially follows that

$$
g ^ {k} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) = \nabla f (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f (x ^ {*}) = \nabla f (x ^ {k}),
$$

$g_{i}^{k} = \bar{g}_{i}^{k}$  , and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| g _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} \frac {2 l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \langle \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}), x ^ {k} - x ^ {*} \rangle\right) = 2 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right), \\ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} = \| \nabla f (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \overset {(1 1)} {\leq} 2 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

applying theorem g.1 we get the following result.

theorem j.3. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  and  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex. then ec-gdstar satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
a = a ^ {\prime} = l, \quad \widetilde {a} = 0, \quad b _ {1} = b _ {2} = \widetilde {b} _ {1} = \widetilde {b} _ {2} = b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 0,
$$

$$
d _ {1} = \widetilde {d} _ {1} = d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \equiv \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0,
$$

$$
\rho_ {1} = \rho_ {2} = 1, \quad c _ {1} = c _ {2} = 0, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \quad f _ {1} = f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = 0,
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {3}}
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \frac {\gamma \mu}{2}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma},
$$

# algorithm 6 ec-sgd-diana

input: learning rates  $\gamma > 0$ ,  $\alpha \in (0,1]$ , initial vectors  $x^0, h_1^0, \ldots, h_n^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$

1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$

2: set  $h^0 = \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} h_i^0$

3: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do

4: broadcast  $x^{k}, h^{k}$  to all workers

5: for  $i = 1,\ldots ,n$  in parallel do

6: sample  $\hat{g}_i^k$  such that  $\mathbf{e}[\hat{g}_i^k\mid x^k] = \nabla f_i(x^k)$  and  $\mathbf{e}\left[\| \hat{g}_i^k -\nabla f_i(x^k)\| ^2\mid x^k\right]\leq \widetilde{d}_{1,i}$  independently from other workers

7:  $g_{i}^{k} = \hat{g}_{i}^{k} - h_{i}^{k} + h^{k}$

8:  $v_{i}^{k} = c(e_{i}^{k} + \gamma g_{i}^{k})$

9:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$

0:  $h_i^{k + 1} = h_i^k +\alpha q(\hat{g}_i^k -h_i^k)$

11: end for

12:  $e^k = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n} e_i^k$ ,  $g^k = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n} g_i^k$ ,  $v^k = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n} v_i^k$ ,  $h^{k+1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n} h_i^{k+1} = h^k +$

$$
\alpha \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} q \left(\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k}\right)
$$

13:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -v^k$

14: end for

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{k \gamma}
$$

when  $\mu = 0$

in other words, ec-gdstar converges with linear rate  $\mathcal{o}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\delta}\ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$  to the exact solution when  $\mu >0$  removing the drawback of ec-sgd and ec-gd. if  $\mu = 0$  then the rate of convergence is  $\mathcal{o}\left(\frac{l\|x^0 - x^*\|^2}{\delta\varepsilon}\right)$ . however, ec-gdstar relies on the fact that  $i$ -th node knows  $\nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$  which is not realistic.

# j.4 ec-sgd-diana

in this section we present a new method that converges to the exact optimum asymptotically but does not need to know  $\nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$  and instead of this it learns the gradients at the optimum. this method is inspired by another method called diana (see [37, 19]).

we notice that master needs to gather only  $c(e_i^k + \gamma g_i^k)$  and  $q(\hat{g}_i^k - h_i^k)$  from all nodes in order to perform an update.

lemma j.3. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \ldots, n$ . then, for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \nabla f \left(x ^ {k}\right), \tag {98}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}, \tag {99}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq \widetilde {d} _ {1}, \tag {100}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 2 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {\widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n} \tag {101}
$$

where  $\widetilde{d}_1 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n\widetilde{d}_{1,i}$  and  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n\|h_i^k - \nabla f(x^*)\|^2$ .

proof. first of all, we show unbiasedness of  $g^k$ :

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} + h ^ {k}\right) = \nabla f (x ^ {k}).
$$

next, we derive the upper bound for  $\| \bar{g}_i^k\| ^2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} = \left\| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} - h ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad 2 \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} + 2 \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \left(h ^ {k} + \nabla f (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \left(f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \langle \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right), x ^ {k} - x ^ {*} \rangle\right) \\ + 2 \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \left(h ^ {k} + \nabla f (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

summing up previous inequality for  $i = 1,\ldots ,n$  we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq 4 l (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \left(\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}))\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(3 4)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2}. \tag {102} \\ \end{array}
$$
