using the unbiasedness of  $\hat{g}_i^k$  we derive

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \widetilde {d} _ {1, i} = \widetilde {d} _ {1}.
$$

finally, we obtain the upper bound for the second moment of  $g^{k}$  using the independence of  $\hat{g}_1^k,\dots ,\hat{g}_n^k$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \stackrel {(3 4)} {=} \| \nabla f (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} + \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} - \nabla f (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} 2 l (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})) + \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k})) \right\| ^ {2} | x ^ {k} \right] \\ = 2 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \leq \quad 2 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \widetilde {d} _ {1, i}. \\ \end{array}
$$

lemma j.4. let assumptions of lemma j.3 hold and  $\alpha \leq 1 / (\omega +1)$ . then, for all  $k\geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - \alpha) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 l \alpha \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \widetilde {d} _ {1}, \tag {103}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|h_i^k - \nabla f_i(x^*)\|^2$  and  $\widetilde{d}_1 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{d}_{1,i}$ .

proof. for simplicity, we introduce new notation:  $h_i^* \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \nabla f_i(x^*)$ . using this we derive an upper bound for the second moment of  $h_i^{k+1} - h_i^*$ :

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k + 1} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} + \alpha q (\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(2 6)} {=} \quad \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + 2 \alpha \langle h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*}, \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \rangle \\ + \alpha^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| q (\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(2 6), (3 5)} {\leq} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} + 2 \alpha \langle h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*}, \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \rangle \\ + \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

using variance decomposition (34) and  $\alpha \leq 1 / (\omega +1)$  we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \stackrel {(3 4)} {=} \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \widetilde {d} _ {1, i} + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

putting all together we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| h _ {i} ^ {k + 1} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \alpha \left\langle \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k}, f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) + h _ {i} ^ {k} - 2 h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\rangle + \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \widetilde {d} _ {1, i} \\ \stackrel {(2 8)} {=} \quad \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} - \alpha \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \widetilde {d} _ {1, i} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} \quad (1 - \alpha) \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + 2 l \alpha \left(f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \langle \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right), x ^ {k} - x ^ {*} \rangle\right) \\ + \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \widetilde {d} _ {1, i}, \\ \end{array}
$$

summing up the above inequality for  $i = 1, \ldots, n$  we derive

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| h _ {i} ^ {k + 1} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq \frac {1 - \alpha}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + 2 l \alpha (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})) + \frac {\alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1)}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \widetilde {d} _ {1, i}.
$$

![](images/f4a9991b9b5c22f599519a625ce085294bcd960a42d0155bef54ae4199736345.jpg)

applying theorem g.1 we get the following result.

theorem j.4. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  and  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex. then ec-sgd-diana satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
a = 2 l, \quad \widetilde {a} = 0, \quad a ^ {\prime} = l, \quad b _ {1} = 2, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \widetilde {d} _ {1, i}, \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} = \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} = \widetilde {b} _ {1} = \widetilde {b} _ {2} = 0, \quad \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0, \quad \rho_ {1} = \alpha , \quad \rho_ {2} = 1, \quad c _ {1} = l \alpha , \quad c _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {1} = 0, \\ d _ {2} = \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \widetilde {d} _ {1}, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {d _ {1}}{n}, \quad g = 0, \\ f _ {1} = \frac {9 6 l \gamma^ {2}}{\delta^ {2} \alpha \left(1 - \operatorname* {m i n} \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2} , \frac {\alpha}{4} \right\}\right)}, \quad f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \left(\frac {4 \alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right) \widetilde {d} _ {1}, \\ \end{array}
$$

with  $\gamma$  and  $\alpha$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {\delta \sqrt {1 - \alpha}}{8 l \sqrt {6 (3 - \alpha)}} \right\}, \quad \alpha \leq \frac {1}{\omega + 1}, \quad m _ {1} = m _ {2} = 0
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2}, \frac {\alpha}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \left(\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + d _ {3}\right),
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \left(\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma k} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + d _ {3}\right)
$$

when  $\mu = 0$

in other words, if

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {\delta \sqrt {1 - \alpha}}{8 l \sqrt {6 (3 - \alpha)}} \right\}, \quad \alpha = \min \left\{\frac {1}{\omega + 1}, \frac {1}{2} \right\}
$$

and  $\widetilde{d}_1 = 0$ , i.e.,  $\hat{g}_i^k = \nabla f_i(x^k)$  almost surely (this is the setup of ec-gd-diana), ec-sgd-diana converges with the linear rate

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\left(\omega + \frac {\kappa}{\bar {\delta}}\right) \ln \frac {1}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

to the exact solution. applying lemma d.2 we establish the rate of convergence to  $\varepsilon$ -solution in the case when  $\mu > 0$ .

corollary j.6. let the assumptions of theorem j.4 hold and  $\mu > 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-sgd-diana with the stepsize

$$
\begin{array}{l} \gamma_ {0} = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {\delta \sqrt {1 - \alpha}}{8 l \sqrt {6 (3 - \alpha)}} \right\}, r _ {0} = \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \|, \tilde {f} _ {1} = \frac {7 8 4 l \gamma^ {2}}{7 \delta^ {2} \alpha}, \\ \gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \min \left\{\frac {n \left(r _ {0} ^ {2} + \widetilde {f} _ {1} \gamma_ {0} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2}\right) \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}{\widetilde {d} _ {1}} , \frac {\delta \left(r _ {0} ^ {2} + \widetilde {f} _ {1} \gamma_ {0} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2}\right) \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{6 l \widetilde {d} _ {1} (4 \alpha (\omega + 1) / \delta + 1)} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

and  $\alpha \leq \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {l}{\delta} r _ {0} ^ {2} \exp \left(- \min \left\{\frac {\delta \mu}{l}, \alpha \right\} k\right) + \frac {\widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \mu k} + \frac {l \widetilde {d} _ {1} \left(\alpha (\omega + 1) / \delta + 1\right)}{\delta \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd-diana requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {1}{\alpha} + \frac {l}{\delta \mu} + \frac {d _ {1}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1} \left(\frac {\alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right)}}{\mu \sqrt {\delta \varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

in particular, if  $\alpha = \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$ , then to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd-diana requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\omega + \frac {l}{\delta \mu} + \frac {\widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1}}}{\delta \mu \sqrt {\varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s,
$$

and if  $\alpha = \frac{\delta}{\omega + 1}$ , then to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd-diana requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\omega + 1}{\delta} + \frac {l}{\delta \mu} + \frac {\widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1}}}{\mu \sqrt {\delta \varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary j.7. let the assumptions of theorem j.4 hold and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-sgd-diana with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {\delta \sqrt {1 - \alpha}}{8 l \sqrt {6 (3 - \alpha)}} \right\}, r _ {0} = \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \|,
$$

$$
\gamma = \min  \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \delta^ {2} \alpha \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma_ {0} \mu}{2} , \frac {\alpha}{4} \right\}\right)}{9 6 l \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt {\frac {n r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\widetilde {d} _ {1} k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {\delta r _ {0} ^ {2}}{6 l \widetilde {d} _ {1} \left(\frac {4 \alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right) k}} \right\},
$$

and  $\alpha \leq \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta k} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{k \sqrt [ 3 ]{\delta^ {2} \alpha}} + \sqrt {\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n k}} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {4} \widetilde {d} _ {1} \left(\frac {\alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right)}{\delta k ^ {2}}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd-diana requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\varepsilon \sqrt [ 3 ]{\delta^ {2} \alpha}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1} \left(\frac {\alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right)}}{\sqrt {\delta \varepsilon^ {3}}}\right)
$$

# algorithm 7 ec-sgdsr-diana

input: learning rates  $\gamma > 0$ ,  $\alpha \in (0,1]$ , initial vectors  $x^0, h_1^0, \ldots, h_n^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$

1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  
2: set  $h^0 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_i^0$  
3: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do  
4: broadcast  $x^{k}, h^{k}$  to all workers  
5: for  $i = 1,\ldots ,n$  in parallel do  
6: sample  $\hat{g}_i^k = \nabla f_{\xi_i^k}(x^k)$  satisfying assumption i.1 independently from other workers  
7:  $g_{i}^{k} = \hat{g}_{i}^{k} - h_{i}^{k} + h^{k}$  
8:  $v_{i}^{k} = c(e_{i}^{k} + \gamma g_{i}^{k})$  
9:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$  
10:  $h_i^{k + 1} = h_i^k +\alpha q(\hat{g}_i^k -h_i^k)$ $\triangleright q(\cdot)$  is calculated independently from other workers  
11: end for  
12:  $e^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}e_{i}^{k},g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k},v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k},h^{k + 1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}h_{i}^{k + 1} = h^{k} +$

$$
\alpha \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} q \left(\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k}\right)
$$

13:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -v^k$

14: end for

iterations. in particular, if  $\alpha = \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$ , then to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd-diana requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} (\omega + 1) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\varepsilon \sqrt [ 3 ]{\delta^ {2}}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1}}}{\delta \sqrt {\varepsilon^ {3}}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s,
$$

and if  $\alpha = \frac{\delta}{\omega + 1}$ , then to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgd-diana requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} (\omega + 1) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\delta \varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1}}}{\sqrt {\delta \varepsilon^ {3}}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

# j.5 ec-sgdsr-diana

in this section we consider the same setup as in section i.1 and consider ec-sgd-diana adjusted to this setup. the resulting algorithm is called ec-sgdsr-diana, see

lemma j.5. let assumption i.1 be satisfied and  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ . then, for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \nabla f \left(x ^ {k}\right), \tag {104}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}, \tag {105}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 6 (\mathcal {l} + l) (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})) + \widetilde {d} _ {1}, \tag {106}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 \mathcal {l} \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + d _ {1} ^ {\prime} \tag {107}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\| h_i^k -\nabla f(x^*)\| ^2$ $\widetilde{d}_1 = \frac{3}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\mathbf{e}_{\mathcal{d}_i}\left[\| \nabla f_{\xi_i}(x^*) - \nabla f_i(x^*)\| ^2\right]$  and

$$
d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right].
$$

proof. first of all, we show unbiasedness of  $g^k$ :

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} + h ^ {k}\right) = \nabla f \left(x ^ {k}\right).
$$

following the same steps as in the proof of (102) we derive (105). next, we establish (106):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \quad = \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1), (8 9)} {\leq} \quad 6 (\mathcal {l} + l) \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, we obtain the upper bound for the second moment of  $g^{k}$  using the independence of  $\xi_1^k,\ldots ,\xi_n^k$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) + \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + 2 \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(8 9)} {\leq} \quad 4 \mathcal {l} \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/522735e953c044ded2fc569ec98b966701f9ef4b3c920805d50e967120711345.jpg)

lemma j.6. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth, assumption i.1 holds and  $\alpha \leq 1 / (\omega + 1)$ . then, for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - \alpha) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 \alpha (3 \mathcal {l} + 4 l) \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + d _ {2}, \tag {108}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^n\| h_i^k -\nabla f_i(x^*)\|^2$  and  $d_{2} = \alpha^{2}(\omega +1)\widetilde{d}_{1}$

proof. the proof is identical to the proof of lemma i.2 up to the following changes in the notation:  $\omega_{1} = \omega$ ,  $\delta_{i}^{k} = q(\hat{g}_{i}^{k} - h_{i}^{k})$  and  $\hat{\delta}_i^k = \hat{g}_i^k -h_i^k$ .

applying theorem g.1 we get the following result.

theorem j.5. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$ ,  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex and assumption i.1 holds. then ec-sgdsr-diana satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
\begin{array}{l} a = 2 l, \quad \widetilde {a} = 3 (\mathcal {l} + l), \quad a ^ {\prime} = 2 \mathcal {l}, \quad b _ {1} = 2, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\mathcal {d} _ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right], \\ \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} = \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad d _ {1} = 0, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2}{3 n} \widetilde {d} _ {1}, \quad d _ {2} = \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \widetilde {d} _ {1} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\widetilde {b} _ {1} = b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} = \widetilde {b} _ {2} = 0, \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0, \rho_ {1} = \alpha , \rho_ {2} = 1, c _ {1} = 2 \alpha (3 \mathcal {l} + 4 l), c _ {2} = 0,
$$

$$
g = 0, \quad f _ {1} = \frac {9 6 l \gamma^ {2}}{\delta^ {2} \alpha \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2} , \frac {\alpha}{4} \right\}\right)}, \quad f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = \frac {6 l \gamma}{\delta} \left(\frac {4 \alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right) \widetilde {d} _ {1},
$$

with  $\gamma$  and  $\alpha$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 \mathcal {l}}, \frac {\delta}{4 \sqrt {6 l \left(4 l + 3 \delta (\mathcal {l} + l) + \frac {1 6 (3 \mathcal {l} + 4 l)}{1 - \alpha}\right)}} \right\}, \quad \alpha \leq \frac {1}{\omega + 1}, \quad m _ {1} = m _ {2} = 0.
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2}, \frac {\alpha}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \left(\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + d _ {3}\right),
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \left(\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma k} + 4 \gamma \left(d _ {1} ^ {\prime} + d _ {3}\right)
$$

when  $\mu = 0$

applying lemma d.2 we establish the rate of convergence to  $\varepsilon$ -solution in the case when  $\mu > 0$ .

corollary j.8. let the assumptions of theorem j.5 hold and  $\mu > 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-sgdsr-diana with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 \mathcal {l}}, \frac {\delta}{4 \sqrt {6 l \left(4 l + 3 \delta (\mathcal {l} + l) + \frac {1 6 (3 \mathcal {l} + 4 l)}{1 - \alpha}\right)}} \right\},
$$

$$
r _ {0} = \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \|, \tilde {f} _ {1} = \frac {9 6 l \gamma_ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta^ {2} \alpha \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma_ {0} \mu}{2} , \frac {\alpha}{4} \right\}\right)},
$$

$$
\gamma = \min  \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{\frac {3 n \left(r _ {0} ^ {2} + \widetilde {f} _ {1} \gamma_ {0} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2}\right) \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}{2 \widetilde {d} _ {1}} , \frac {\delta \left(r _ {0} ^ {2} + \widetilde {f} _ {1} \gamma_ {0} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2}\right) \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{6 l \widetilde {d} _ {1} \left(\frac {4 \alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right)} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\},
$$

and  $\alpha \leq \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\left(\mathcal {l} + \frac {\sqrt {l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2} \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {\mu}{\mathcal {l} + \frac {\sqrt {l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta}}, \alpha \right\} k\right) + \frac {\widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \mu k} + \frac {l \widetilde {d} _ {1} \left(\frac {\alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right)}{\delta \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right)
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgdsr-diana requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {1}{\alpha} + \frac {\mathcal {l}}{\mu} + \frac {\sqrt {l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta \mu} + \frac {\widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1} \left(\frac {\alpha (\omega + 1)}{\delta} + 1\right)}}{\mu \sqrt {\delta \varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

in particular, if  $\alpha = \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$ , then to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgdsr-diana requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\omega + \frac {\mathcal {l}}{\mu} + \frac {\sqrt {l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta \mu} + \frac {\widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1}}}{\delta \mu \sqrt {\varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s,
$$

and if  $\alpha = \frac{\delta}{\omega + 1}$ , then to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgdsr-diana requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\omega + 1}{\delta} + \frac {\mathcal {l}}{\mu} + \frac {\sqrt {l \mathcal {l}}}{\delta \mu} + \frac {\widetilde {d} _ {1}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \widetilde {d} _ {1}}}{\mu \sqrt {\delta \varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

$$

iterations. in particular, if  $\alpha = \frac{1}{\omega + 1}$ , then to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-sgdsr-diana requires

$$

# j.6 ec-lsvrg

in this section we consider problem (1) with  $f(x)$  being  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex and  $f_{i}(x)$  satisfying (3) where functions  $f_{ij}(x)$  are convex and  $l$ -smooth. for this problem we propose a new method called ec-lsvrg which takes for the origin another method called lsvrg (see [17, 31]).

lemma j.7. for all  $k \geq 0$ ,  $i \in [n]$  we have

$$
\bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} = \mathbf {e} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) \tag {109}
$$

and

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq 4 l (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})) + d _ {1}, \tag {110}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 1 2 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 3 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}, \tag {111}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \tag {112}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^n\sum_{j=1}^n\|\nabla f_{ij}(w_i^k) - \nabla f_{ij}(x^*)\|^2$  and  $d_1 = \frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n\|\nabla f_i(x^*)\|^2$ .

# algorithm 8 ec-lsvrg

input: learning rate  $\gamma > 0$ , initial vector  $x^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$

1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  
2: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do  
3: broadcast  $x^k$  to all workers  
4: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do  
5: pick  $l$  uniformly at random from  $[m]$  
6: set  $g_i^k = \nabla f_{il}(x^k) - \nabla f_{il}(w_i^k) + \nabla f_i(w_i^k)$  
7:  $v_{i}^{k} = c(e_{i}^{k} + \gamma g_{i}^{k})$  
8:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$  
9:  $w_{i}^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^{k}, & \mathrm{with~probability~}p,\\ w_{i}^{k}, & \mathrm{with~probability~}1 - p \end{array} \right.$  
10: end for  
11:  $e^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}e_{i}^{k},g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k},v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k}$  
12:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -v^k$  
13: end for

proof. first of all, we derive unbiasedness of  $g_{i}^{k}$ :

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left(\nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) + \nabla f _ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right)\right) = \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right).
$$

next, we get an upper bound for  $\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\| \vec{g}_i^k\|^2$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \quad = \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} 4 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

using (109) we establish the following inequality:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \quad \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1), (3 4)} {\leq} \quad 1 2 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {3}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, we derive (112):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) + \nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left| \left| \nabla f _ {i l} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i l} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right| \right| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ = \quad \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \frac {1}{m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left(\nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1), (3 4)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/9212538d249b098bccff28eb2e8cd604d7d99a49a5f243ba722e9567a604b94f.jpg)

lemma j.8. for all  $k \geq 0$ ,  $i \in [n]$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - p) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 l p \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right), \tag {113}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{nm}\sum_{i = 1}^n\sum_{j = 1}^n\| \nabla f_{ij}(w_i^k) - \nabla f_{ij}(x^*)\|^2.$

proof. by definition of  $w_{i}^{k + 1}$  we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k + 1}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ = \frac {1 - p}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} + \frac {p}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} \quad (1 - p) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + \frac {2 l p}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} d _ {f _ {i j}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \\ = \left(1 - p\right) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 l p \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/48b9a787fdd90254c96d419bf9b08fc8f8dd7215f15b67333156036b29ead4c1.jpg)

applying theorem g.1 we get the following result.

theorem j.6. assume that  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex and functions  $f_{ij}$  are convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n], j \in [m]$ . then ec-lsvrg satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
a = 2 l, \quad \widetilde {a} = 1 2 l, \quad a ^ {\prime} = 2 l, \quad b _ {1} = \widetilde {b} _ {1} = b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {1} = \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2},
$$

$$
d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \widetilde {d} _ {1} = 0, \quad \widetilde {b} _ {2} = 3, \quad b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 2, \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0, \quad c _ {1} = 0,
$$

$$
\sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} = \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \rho_ {1} = 1, \quad \rho_ {2} = p, \quad c _ {2} = l p, \quad d _ {2} = 0,
$$

$$
g = 0, \quad f _ {1} = 0, \quad f _ {2} = \frac {7 2 l \gamma^ {2}}{\delta p \left(1 - \min \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2} , \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right)}, \quad d _ {3} = \frac {1 2 l \gamma}{\delta^ {2}} d _ {1},
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2 4 l}, \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {3 \left(2 + 3 \delta \left(2 + \frac {1}{1 - p}\right)\right)}} \right\}, \quad m _ {2} = \frac {4}{p}.
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2}, \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \left(t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma} + \frac {4 8 l \gamma^ {2}}{\delta^ {2}} d _ {1}
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \left(t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma k} + \frac {4 8 l \gamma^ {2}}{\delta^ {2}} d _ {1}
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ , where  $t^k \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \|x^k - x^*\|^2 + m_2 \gamma^2 \sigma_k^2$ .

in other words, ec-lsvrg converges with linear rate  $\mathcal{o}\left(\left(\frac{1}{p} +\frac{\kappa}{\delta\sqrt{1 - p}}\right)\ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$  to the neighbourhood of the solution. if  $m\geq 2$  then taking  $p = \frac{1}{m}$  we get that in expectation the sample complexity of one iteration of ec-lsvrg is  $\mathcal{o}(1)$  gradients calculations per node as for ec-sgdsr with standard sampling and the rate of convergence to the neighbourhood becomes  $\mathcal{o}\left((m + \frac{\kappa}{\delta})\ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$ . we notice that the size of this neighbourhood is typically smaller than for ec-sgdsr, but still the method fails to converge to the exact solution with linear rate. applying lemma d.2 we establish the rate of convergence to  $\varepsilon$ -solution in the case when  $\mu >0$ .

corollary j.10. let the assumptions of theorem j.6 hold and  $\mu > 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-lsvrg with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min \left\{\frac {1}{2 4 l}, \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {3 \left(2 + 3 \delta \left(2 + \frac {1}{1 - p}\right)\right)}} \right\},
$$

$$
\tilde {t} ^ {0} = \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + m _ {2} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}, \quad \tilde {f} _ {2} = \frac {7 2 l \gamma_ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta p \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma_ {0} \mu}{2} , \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right)},
$$

$$
\gamma = \min  \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \frac {\delta^ {2} \left(\tilde {t ^ {0}} + \tilde {f} _ {2} \gamma_ {0} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right) \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{4 8 l d _ {1}} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\},
$$

and  $p = \frac{1}{m}$ ,  $m \geq 2$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {l}{\delta} \left(\tilde {t} ^ {0} + \tilde {f} _ {2} \gamma_ {0} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right) \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {\delta \mu}{l}, \frac {1}{m} \right\} k\right) + \frac {l d _ {1}}{\delta^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-lsvrg requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(m + \frac {l}{\delta \mu} + \frac {\sqrt {l d _ {1}}}{\delta \mu \sqrt {\varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary j.11. let the assumptions of theorem j.6 hold and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-lsvrg with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2 4 l}, \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {3 \left(2 + 3 \delta \left(2 + \frac {1}{1 - p}\right)\right)}} \right\}, r _ {0} = \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \|,
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} p}{4 \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \delta p \left(1 - \min \left\lbrace \frac {\gamma_ {0} \mu}{2} , \frac {p}{4} \right\rbrace\right)}{7 2 l \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {\delta^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2}}{1 2 l d _ {1} k}} \right\},
$$

# algorithm 9 ec-lsvrgstar

input: learning rate  $\gamma > 0$ , initial vector  $x^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$

1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  
2: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do  
3: broadcast  $x^k$  to all workers  
4: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do  
5: pick  $l$  uniformly at random from  $[m]$  
6: set  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{il}(x^{k}) - \nabla f_{il}(w_{i}^{k}) + \nabla f_{i}(w_{i}^{k}) - \nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$  
7:  $v_{i}^{k} = c(e_{i}^{k} + \gamma g_{i}^{k})$  
8:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$  
9:  $w_{i}^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^{k}, & \mathrm{with~probability~}p,\\ w_{i}^{k}, & \mathrm{with~probability~}1 - p \end{array} \right.$  
10: end for  
11:  $e^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}e_{i}^{k},g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k},v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k}$  
12:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -v^k$  
13: end for

and  $p = \frac{1}{m}$ ,  $m \geq 2$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta k} + \frac {\sqrt {m r _ {0} ^ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{k} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\sqrt [ 3 ]{\delta k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{(\delta k) ^ {2 / 3}} \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-lsvrg requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {m r _ {0} ^ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\sqrt [ 3 ]{\delta} \varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta \varepsilon^ {3 / 2}} \sqrt {\frac {l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}}\right)
$$

iterations.

# j.7 ec-lsvrgstar

in the setup of section j.6 we now assume that  $i$ -th node has an access to the  $\nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$ . under this unrealistic assumption we construct the method called ec-lsvrgstar that asymptotically converges to the exact solution.

lemma j.9. for all  $k \geq 0$ ,  $i \in [n]$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \nabla f \left(x ^ {k}\right) \tag {114}
$$

and

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq 2 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right), \tag {115}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}, \tag {116}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}, \tag {117}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{nm}\sum_{i = 1}^n\sum_{j = 1}^n\| \nabla f_{ij}(w_i^k) - \nabla f_{ij}(x^*)\|^2.$

proof. first of all, we derive unbiasedness of  $g^k$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) + \nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left(\nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) + \nabla f _ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \\ { = } { \nabla f ( x ^ { k } ) + \frac { 1 } { n } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n } \left( - \nabla f _ { i } ( w _ { i } ^ { k } ) + \nabla f _ { i } ( w _ { i } ^ { k } ) \right) - \nabla f ( x ^ { * } ) = \nabla f ( x ^ { k } ) . } \\ \end{array}
$$

next, we get an upper bound for  $\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\| \bar{g}_i^k\|^2$ :

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} 2 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right).
$$

since the variance of random vector is not greater than its second moment we obtain:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \quad \stackrel {(3 4)} {\leq} \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 1), (3 4)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

inequality (117) trivially follows from the inequality above by jensen's inequality and convexity of  $\| \cdot \| ^2$ .

lemma j.10. for all  $k \geq 0$ ,  $i \in [n]$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - p) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 l p \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right), \tag {118}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{1}{nm}\sum_{i = 1}^n\sum_{j = 1}^n\| \nabla f_{ij}(w_i^k) - \nabla f_{ij}(x^*)\|^2.$

proof. the proof of this lemma is identical to the proof of lemma j.8.  $\square$

![](images/a8438fc3681c96daaca2b20e46a31d415d1f645427ae4bc7a11ed642f8aa8862.jpg)

applying theorem g.1 we get the following result.

theorem j.7. assume that  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex and functions  $f_{ij}$  are convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n], j \in [m]$ . then ec-lsvrgstar satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
a = l, \quad \widetilde {a} = a ^ {\prime} = 2 l, \quad b _ {1} = \widetilde {b} _ {1} = b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} = 0, \quad \widetilde {b} _ {2} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 2, \quad d _ {1} = d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = 0,
$$

$$
\sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0,, \quad c _ {1} = 0, \quad \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} = \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \rho_ {1} = 1,
$$

$$
\rho_ {2} = p, \quad c _ {2} = l p, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \quad g = 0, \quad f _ {1} = 0, \quad f _ {2} = \frac {4 8 l \gamma^ {2} (2 + p)}{\delta p}, \quad d _ {3} = 0,
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {3}{5 6 l}, \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {3 \left(1 + \delta \left(1 + \frac {2}{1 - p}\right)\right)}} \right\}, \quad m _ {2} = \frac {8}{3 p}.
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2}, \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 (t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2})}{\gamma}
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \left(t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma k}
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ , where  $t^k \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \|x^k - x^*\|^2 + m_2 \gamma^2 \sigma_k^2$ .

in other words, ec-lsvrgstar converges with linear rate  $\mathcal{o}\left(\left(\frac{1}{p} +\frac{\kappa}{\delta\sqrt{1 - p}}\right)\ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$  exactly to the solution when  $\mu >0$ . if  $m\geq 2$  then taking  $p = \frac{1}{m}$  we get that in expectation the sample complexity of one iteration of ec-lsvrgstar is  $\mathcal{o}(1)$  gradients calculations per node as for ec-sgdsr with standard sampling and the rate of convergence becomes  $\mathcal{o}\left((m + \frac{\kappa}{\delta})\ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$ .

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary j.12. let the assumptions of theorem j.7 hold and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-lsvrgstar with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {3}{5 6 l}, \frac {\delta}{8 l \sqrt {3 \left(1 + \delta \left(1 + \frac {2}{1 - p}\right)\right)}} \right\}, r _ {0} = \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \|,
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {3 p r _ {0} ^ {2}}{8 \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \delta p \left(1 - \min \left\{\frac {\gamma_ {0} \mu}{2} , \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right)}{7 2 l \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}} \right\},
$$

and  $p = \frac{1}{m}$ ,  $m \geq 2$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta k} + \frac {\sqrt {r _ {0} ^ {2} m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{k} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\sqrt [ 3 ]{\delta} k}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-lsvrgstar requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {r _ {0} ^ {2} m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}{\sqrt [ 3 ]{\delta} \varepsilon}\right)
$$

iterations.

however, such convergence guarantees are obtained under very restrictive assumption: the method requires to know vectors  $\nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$ .

# j.8 ec-lsvrg-diana

in the setup of section j.6 we construct a new method called ec-lsvrg-diana which does not require to know  $\nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$  and has linear convergence to the exact solution. as in ec-sgd-diana the master needs to gather only  $c(e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k)$  and  $q(\hat{g}_i^k -h_i^k)$  from all nodes in order to perform an update.

lemma j.11. assume that  $f_{ij}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \ldots, n$ ,  $j = 1, \ldots, m$ . then, for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \nabla f \left(x ^ {k}\right), \tag {119}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2}, \tag {120}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 6 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 3 \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} + 3 \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2}, \tag {121}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \tag {122}
$$

where

$$
\sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}.
$$

proof. first of all, we show unbiasedness of  $g^k$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k} + h ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left(\nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) + \nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} + h ^ {k}\right) = \nabla f (x ^ {k}). \\ \end{array}
$$

# algorithm 10 ec-lsvrg-diana

input: learning rates  $\gamma > 0$ ,  $\alpha \in (0,1]$ , initial vectors  $x^0, h_1^0, \ldots, h_n^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$

1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  
2: set  $h^0 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_i^0$  
3: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do  
4: broadcast  $x^{k}, h^{k}$  to all workers  
5: for  $i = 1,\ldots ,n$  in parallel do  
pick  $l$  uniformly at random from  $[m]$  
7: set  $\hat{g}_i^k = \nabla f_{il}(x^k) - \nabla f_{il}(w_i^k) + \nabla f_i(w_i^k)$  
8:  $g_{i}^{k} = \hat{g}_{i}^{k} - h_{i}^{k} + h^{k}$  
9:  $v_{i}^{k} = c(e_{i}^{k} + \gamma g_{i}^{k})$  
10:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$  
11:  $h_i^{k + 1} = h_i^k +\alpha q(\hat{g}_i^k -h_i^k)$  
12:  $w_{i}^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^{k}, & \text{with probability } p,\\ w_{i}^{k}, & \text{with probability } 1. \end{array} \right.$  
13: end for  
14:  $e^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}e_{i}^{k},g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k},v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k},h^{k + 1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}h_{i}^{k + 1} = h^{k} +$

$$
\alpha \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} q \left(\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k}\right)
$$

15:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -v^k$  
16: end for

next, we derive the upper bound for  $\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\| \bar{g}_i^k\|^2$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \quad = \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} + h ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \left(h ^ {k} - \nabla f (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1), (3 4)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

since the variance of random vector is not greater than its second moment we obtain:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \quad \stackrel {(3 4)} {\leq} \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) + \nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} + h ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left| \left| \nabla f _ {i l} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i l} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right| \right| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \left(h ^ {k} - \nabla f (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1), (3 4)} {\leq} \quad 6 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {3}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {3}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, we obtain an upper bound for the second moment of  $g^k$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \quad = \quad \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) + \nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {i l} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i l} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ = \quad \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \frac {1}{m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left(\nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1), (3 4)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/8571a693f07d40e251aba4d086d6e7f911480042a870fc0722dabb6f4fa0d31c.jpg)

lemma j.12. assume that  $\alpha \leq 1 / (\omega +1)$ . then, for all  $k\geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {1, k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - \alpha) \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} + 6 l \alpha \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \alpha \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2}, \tag {123}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {2, k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - p) \sigma_ {k, 2} ^ {2} + 2 l p \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \tag {124}
$$

where  $\sigma_{1,k}^2 = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|h_i^k - \nabla f_i(x^*)\|^2$  and  $\sigma_{2,k}^2 = \frac{1}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|\nabla f_{ij}(w_i^k) - \nabla f_{ij}(x^*)\|^2$ .

proof. first of all, we derive an upper bound for the second moment of  $h_i^{k + 1} - h_i^*$ :

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k + 1} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} + \alpha q (\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(2 6)} {=} \quad \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + 2 \alpha \langle h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*}, \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \rangle \\ + \alpha^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| q (\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(2 6), (3 5)} {\leq} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} + 2 \alpha \langle h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*}, \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \rangle \\ + \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

using variance decomposition (34) and  $\alpha \leq 1 / (\omega +1)$  we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \stackrel {(3 4)} {=} \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] + \alpha^ {2} (\omega + 1) \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \alpha \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} 2 \alpha \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + 2 \alpha {\bf e} \left[ \left| \left| \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right| \right| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(3 4)} {\leq} \quad 2 \alpha \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + 2 \alpha \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {i l} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i l} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} | x ^ {k} \right] + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \alpha d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) + \frac {2 \alpha}{m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

putting all together we obtain

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| h _ {i} ^ {k + 1} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \alpha \left\langle \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - h _ {i} ^ {k}, f _ {i} \left(x ^ {k}\right) + h _ {i} ^ {k} - 2 h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\rangle
$$

$$
+ 4 l \alpha d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) + \frac {2 \alpha}{m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right\| ^ {2}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(2 8)} {=} \left\| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2} + \alpha \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} - \alpha \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \\ + 4 l \alpha d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) + \frac {2 \alpha}{m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} \quad (1 - \alpha) \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - h _ {i} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + 6 l \alpha d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \\ + \frac {2 \alpha}{m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

summing up the above inequality for  $i = 1, \ldots, n$  we derive

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {1, k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq (1 - \alpha) \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} + 6 l \alpha \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 \alpha \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2}.
$$

similarly to the proof of lemma j.8 we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {2, k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k + 1}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ = \frac {1 - p}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {p}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 1)} {\leq} (1 - p) \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} + \frac {2 l p}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} d _ {f _ {i j}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \\ = \quad (1 - p) \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} + 2 l p \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/78ad7317492d4b8c2649f89cc9186ef93a0970ba972303d323791a4d8a824b78.jpg)

applying theorem g.1 we get the following result.

theorem j.8. assume that  $f_{ij}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$ ,  $j = 1, \dots, m$  and  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex. then ec-lsvrg-diana satisfies assumption 3.3 with

$$
a = a ^ {\prime} = 2 l, \quad b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} = 0, \quad b _ {1} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 2, \quad d _ {1} = \widetilde {d} _ {1} = d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = d _ {2} = d _ {3} = 0,
$$

$$
\widetilde {a} = 3 l, \quad \widetilde {b} _ {1} = \widetilde {b} _ {2} = 3, \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| h _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \rho_ {1} = \alpha ,
$$

$$
\sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \rho_ {2} = p, \quad c _ {1} = 3 l \alpha , \quad c _ {2} = l p,
$$

$$
g = 2, \quad f _ {1} = \frac {2 4 l \gamma^ {2} \left(\frac {4}{\delta} + 3\right)}{\delta \alpha \left(1 - \min \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2} , \frac {\alpha}{4} , \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right)}, \quad f _ {2} = \frac {2 4 l \gamma^ {2} \left(\frac {4}{1 - \alpha} \left(\frac {4}{\delta} + 3\right) + 3\right)}{\delta p \left(1 - \min \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2} , \frac {\alpha}{4} , \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right)},
$$

with  $\gamma$  and  $\alpha$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {9}{2 9 6 l}, \frac {\delta}{4 l \sqrt {6 \left(4 + 3 \delta + \frac {2}{1 - \alpha} \left(3 + \frac {4}{1 - p}\right) (4 + 3 \delta) + \frac {6 \delta}{1 - p}\right)}} \right\}, \quad \alpha \leq \frac {1}{\omega + 1}
$$

with  $m_1 = 0$  and  $m_2 = \frac{8}{3p} + \frac{32}{9p}$  and for all  $k \geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma \mu}{2}, \frac {\alpha}{4}, \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \left(t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}\right)}{\gamma},
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \left(t ^ {0} + \gamma f _ {1} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \gamma f _ {2} \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}\right)}{k \gamma}
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ , where  $t^k \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \|x^k - x^*\|^2 + m_2 \gamma^2 \sigma_{2,k}^2$ .

in other words, if  $p = 1 / m$ ,  $m \geq 2$  and

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {9}{2 9 6 l}, \frac {\delta}{4 l \sqrt {6 \left(4 + 3 \delta + \frac {2}{1 - \alpha} \left(3 + \frac {4}{1 - p}\right) (4 + 3 \delta) + \frac {6 \delta}{1 - p}\right)}} \right\}, \quad \alpha = \min \left\{\frac {1}{\omega + 1}, \frac {1}{2} \right\},
$$

then ec-lsvrg-diana converges with the linear rate

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\left(\omega + m + \frac {\kappa}{\delta}\right) \ln \frac {1}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

to the exact solution when  $\mu > 0$ .

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary j.13. let the assumptions of theorem j.8 hold and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of ec-lsvrg-diana with the stepsize

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {9}{2 9 6 l}, \frac {\delta}{4 l \sqrt {6 \left(4 + 3 \delta + \frac {2}{1 - \alpha} \left(3 + \frac {4}{1 - p}\right) (4 + 3 \delta) + \frac {6 \delta}{1 - p}\right)}} \right\}, r _ {0} = \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \|,
$$

$$
\gamma = \min  \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {9 p r _ {0} ^ {2}}{5 6 \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\frac {2 4 l \left(\frac {4}{\delta} + 3\right)}{\delta \alpha \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma_ {0} \mu}{2} , \frac {\alpha}{4} , \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right)} \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + \frac {2 4 l \left(\frac {4}{1 - \alpha} \left(\frac {4}{\delta} + 3\right) + 3\right)}{\delta p \left(1 - \min  \left\{\frac {\gamma_ {0} \mu}{2} , \frac {\alpha}{4} , \frac {p}{4} \right\}\right)} \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}} \right\},
$$

and  $p = \frac{1}{m}$ ,  $m \geq 2$ ,  $\alpha = \min \left\{\frac{1}{\omega + 1}, \frac{1}{2}\right\}$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta k} + \frac {\sqrt {r _ {0} ^ {2} m \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}}{k} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} ((\omega + 1) \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + m \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2})}}{\delta^ {2 / 3} k}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  ec-lsvrg-diana requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\delta \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {r _ {0} ^ {2} m \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2}}}{\varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} ((\omega + 1) \sigma_ {1 , 0} ^ {2} + m \sigma_ {2 , 0} ^ {2})}}{\delta^ {2 / 3} \varepsilon}\right)
$$

iterations.

table 4: complexity of sgd methods with delayed updates established in this paper. symbols:  $\varepsilon =$  error tolerance;  $\delta =$  contraction factor of compressor  $\mathcal{c}$ ;  $\omega =$  variance parameter of compressor  $\mathcal{q}$ ;  $\kappa = l / \mu$ ;  $\mathcal{l} =$  expected smoothness constant;  $\sigma_{*}^{2} =$  variance of the stochastic gradients in the solution;  $\zeta_{*}^{2} =$  average of  $\| \nabla f_{i}(x^{*})\|^{2}$ ;  $\sigma^2 =$  average of the uniform bounds for the variances of stochastic gradients of workers;  $\mathcal{m}_{2,q} = (\omega +1)\sigma^2 +\omega \zeta_*^2;$ $\sigma_q^2 = (1 + \omega)\left(1 + \frac{\omega}{n}\right)\sigma^2.$ $^\dagger$ d-qgdstar is a special case of d-qsgdstar where each worker  $i$  computes the full gradient  $\nabla f_i(x^k)$ ;  $^\ddagger$ d-gd-diana is a special case of d-sgd-diana where each worker  $i$  computes the full gradient  $\nabla f_i(x^k)$ .

# algorithm 11 d-sgd

input: learning rate  $\gamma > 0$ , initial vector  $x^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$

1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  
2: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do  
3: broadcast  $x^k$  to all workers  
4: for  $i = 1,\ldots ,n$  in parallel do  
5: sample  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{\xi_{i}}(x^{k}) - \nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$  
6:  $v_{i}^{k} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}\gamma g_{i}^{k - \tau}, & \text{if} k\geq \tau ,\\ 0, & \text{if} k <   \tau \end{array} \right.$  
7:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$  
8: end for  
9:  $e^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}e_{i}^{k},g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k},v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\nabla f_{\xi_{i}}(x^{k - \tau})$  
10:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -v^k$  
11: end for

# k special cases: delayed updates methods

# k.1 d-sgd

in this section we consider the same setup as in section j.2. we notice that vectors  $e_i^k$  appear only in the analysis and there is no need to compute them. moreover, we use  $\nabla f_i(x^*)$  in the definition of  $g_i^k$  which is problematic at the first glance. indeed, workers do not know  $\nabla f_i(x^*)$ . however, since  $0 = \nabla f(x^*) = \frac{1}{n} \nabla f_i(x^*)$  and master node uses averages of  $g_i^k$  for the updates one can ignore  $\nabla f_i(x^*)$  in  $g_i^k$  in the implementation of d-sgd and get exactly the same method. we define  $g_i^k$  in such a way only for the theoretical analysis.

lemma k.1 (see also lemmas 1,2 from [39]). assume that  $f_{\xi_i}(x)$  are convex in  $x$  for every  $\xi_i$ ,  $i = 1, \ldots, n$ . then for every  $x \in \mathbb{r}^d$  and  $i = 1, \ldots, n$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right]. \tag {125}
$$

if further  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex with possibly non-convex  $f_{i}, f_{\xi_{i}}$  and  $\mu > 0$ , then for every  $x \in \mathbb{r}^d$  and  $i = 1, \ldots, n$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 l \kappa \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right], \tag {126}
$$

where  $\kappa = \frac{l}{\mu}$

proof. by definition of  $g^k$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) + \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad 2 \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + 2 \underbrace {\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right]} _ {\mathrm {v a r} \left[ \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \right]} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(3 1)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \underbrace {\mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right]} _ {\operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right]} \tag {127} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last inequality we use independence of  $\nabla f_{\xi_i}(x^*)$ ,  $i = 1, \ldots, n$ . using this we derive inequality (125):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \stackrel {(1 2 7), (1 1)} {\leq} \frac {4 l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {\xi_ {i}}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \mid x ^ {k} \right] + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \\ = \quad \frac {4 l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \\ = \quad 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

next, if  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -quasi strongly convex, but  $f_{i}, f_{\xi_{i}}$  are not necessary convex, we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \overset {(1 2 7), (1 0)} {\leq} \frac {2 l ^ {2}}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| x ^ {k} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \right] \\ \stackrel {(9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {4 l ^ {2}}{\mu} \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/5bc8edf26f8b38833f7b48c32c7b1b30f2f6d1e3d89f5f817ce61a56638d2938.jpg)

theorem k.1. assume that  $f_{\xi}(x)$  is convex in  $x$  for every  $\xi$ . then d-sgd satisfies assumption 3.4 with

$$
a ^ {\prime} = 2 l, \quad b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathrm {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \right], \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \equiv \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0
$$

$$
\rho_ {1} = \rho_ {2} = 1, \quad c _ {1} = c _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0
$$

$$
f _ {1} = f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = \frac {6 \gamma \tau l}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right]
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \frac {1}{8 l \sqrt {2 \tau (\tau + 2)}}
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \frac {\gamma \mu}{2}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma} + \frac {8 \gamma}{n ^ {2}} \left(1 + 3 l \gamma \tau\right) \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathrm {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \right]
$$

when  $\mu > 0$  and

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma k} + \frac {8 \gamma}{n ^ {2}} \left(1 + 3 l \gamma \tau\right) \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \right]
$$

when  $\mu = 0$ . if further  $f_{i}(x)$  are  $\mu$ -strongly convex with possibly non-convex  $f_{\xi_i}$  and  $\mu > 0$ , then d-sgd satisfies assumption 3.4 with

$$
a ^ {\prime} = 2 \kappa l, \quad b _ {1} ^ {\prime} = b _ {2} ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathrm {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \right], \quad \sigma_ {1, k} ^ {2} \equiv \sigma_ {2, k} ^ {2} \equiv 0,
$$

$$
\rho_ {1} = \rho_ {2} = 1, \quad c _ {1} = c _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \quad g = 0,
$$

$$
f _ {1} = f _ {2} = 0, \quad d _ {3} = \frac {6 \gamma \tau l}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right]
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{8 \kappa l}, \frac {1}{8 l \sqrt {2 \tau (\tau + 2 \kappa)}} \right\}
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \frac {\gamma \mu}{2}\right) ^ {k} \frac {4 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma} + \frac {8 \gamma}{n ^ {2}} \left(1 + 3 l \gamma \tau\right) \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) \right].
$$

in other words, d-sgd converges with linear rate  $\mathcal{o}\left(\tau \kappa \ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$  to the neighbourhood of the solution when  $\mu > 0$ . applying lemma d.2 we establish the rate of convergence to  $\varepsilon$ -solution.

corollary k.1. let the assumptions of theorem k.1 hold,  $f_{\xi}(x)$  are convex for each  $\xi$  and  $\mu > 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of d-sgd with the stepsize

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{8 l \sqrt {2 \tau (\tau + 2)}}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \min \left\{\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime}} , \frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{3 \tau l d _ {1}} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}
$$

we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(l \tau \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \exp \left(- \frac {\mu}{\tau l} k\right) + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu k} + \frac {l \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  d-sgd requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\tau l}{\mu} + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}}{\mu \sqrt {\varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

corollary k.2. let the assumptions of theorem k.1 hold and  $f(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex with  $\mu > 0$  and possibly non-convex  $f_{i}, f_{\xi_{i}}$ . then after  $k$  iterations of d-sgd with the stepsize

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{8 \kappa l}, \frac {1}{8 l \sqrt {2 \tau (\tau + 2 \kappa)}}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \min \left\{\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime}} , \frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{l \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime}} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}
$$

we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(l (\kappa + \tau \sqrt {\kappa}) \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \exp \left(- \min \left\{\frac {\mu}{\tau l \sqrt {\kappa}}, \frac {1}{\kappa^ {2}} \right\} k\right) + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu k} + \frac {l \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon \mathsf{d - sgd}$  requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\kappa^ {2} + \tau \kappa^ {3 / 2} + \frac {d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\mu \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {l \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}}{\mu \sqrt {\varepsilon}}\right) i t e r a t i o n s.
$$

applying lemma d.3 we get the complexity result in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary k.3. let the assumptions of theorem k.1 hold,  $f_{\xi}(x)$  are convex for each  $\xi$  and  $\mu = 0$ . then after  $k$  iterations of d-sgd with the stepsize

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{8 l \sqrt {2 \tau (\tau + 2)}}, \sqrt {\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{d _ {1} ^ {\prime} k}} , \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{3 l \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime} k}} \right\}
$$

we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\tau l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{k} + \sqrt {\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}}{k ^ {2 / 3}}\right)
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$ . that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\bar{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]\leq \varepsilon$  d-sgd requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\tau l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}{\varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l \tau d _ {1} ^ {\prime}}}{\varepsilon^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

iterations.

# k.2 d-qsgd

in this section we show how one can combine delayed updates with quantization using our scheme.

lemma k.2. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i = 1, \ldots, n$ . then, for all  $k \geq 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \nabla f (x ^ {k}),
$$

$$
{\bf e} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 2 l \left(1 + \frac {2 \omega}{n}\right) \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {(\omega + 1) d}{n} + \frac {2 \omega}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}
$$

where  $d = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}d_{i}$

proof. first of all, we show unbiasedness of  $g^k$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ g ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \mid x ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \mathbf {e} _ {q} \left[ q (\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \right] \mid x ^ {k} \right] \\ \stackrel {(2 6)} {=} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) = \nabla f (x ^ {k}), \\ \end{array}
$$

# algorithm 12 d-qsgd

input: learning rate  $\gamma > 0$ , initial vector  $x^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$

1: set  $e_i^0 = 0$  for all  $i = 1, \dots, n$  
2: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do  
3: broadcast  $x^{k - \tau}$  to all workers  
4: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  do  
5: sample  $\hat{g}_i^{k - \tau}$  independently from other nodes such that  $\mathbf{e}[\hat{g}_i^{k - \tau} \mid x^{k - \tau}] = \nabla f_i(x^{k - \tau})$  and  $\mathbf{e}\left[\| \hat{g}_i^{k - \tau} - \nabla f_i(x^{k - \tau})\|^2 \mid x^{k - \tau}\right] \leq d_i$  
6:  $g_{i}^{k - \tau} = q(\hat{g}_{i}^{k - \tau}) - \nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$  (quantization is performed independently from other nodes)  
7:  $v_{i}^{k} = \gamma g_{i}^{k - \tau}$  8:  $e_i^{k + 1} = e_i^k +\gamma g_i^k -v_i^k$  
9: end for  
10:  $e^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}e_{i}^{k}, g^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k}, v^{k} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}v_{i}^{k} = \frac{\gamma}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k - \tau} = \frac{\gamma}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}g_{i}^{k - \tau}$  
11:  $x^{k + 1} = x^k -v^k$  
12: end for

where  $\mathbf{e}_q[\cdot ]$  denotes mathematical expectation w.r.t. the randomness coming only from the quantization. next, we derive the upper bound for the second moment of  $g^{k}$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} _ {q} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] = \mathbf {e} _ {q} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(q \left(\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(3 4)} {=} \mathbf {e} _ {q} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(q \left(\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] + \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2}. \tag {128} \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $q(\hat{g}_1^k), \ldots, q(\hat{g}_n^k)$  are independent quantizations, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} _ {q} \left[ \| g ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {(1 2 8)} {\leq} \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {q} \left[ \left\| q (\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k}) - \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] + \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(2 6)} {\leq} \quad \frac {\omega}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\hat {g} _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$
